~~**P8-27 · Skipping plays differently from watching (Champions League and World Cup).**~~ When you skip, the "skip" paths for the Champions League league phase and the World Cup group stage (`skipAll` in `CLSimulation` and `WCSimulation`, `app/game/simulation.tsx`) simulate each match with the clubs' base ratings. They skip what the live paths do:

- **Rotation:** no rested players.
- **Availability:** injured or suspended players don't lower the rating.
- **Lineups:** no seeded lineup-based effective rating (`effectiveMatchOvrs`).
- **Order:** the result is decided before the seed exists.
  *(Done 19 September 2026: both paths call one `playFixture` (`src/engine/play-fixture.ts`). The skip was missing more than listed: it also never updated form. Skipping during your live World Cup match also used to replay the matchday on the clock, counting its injuries twice and dropping the match you were watching; it now commits it. `verify-play-fixture` plays the same seeds watched and skipped and compares them, and checks absences, rotation and form reach the fixture.)*

The stored sheets still regenerate consistently, because the skip also stores no rotation. But a skipped phase is a slightly different competition from a watched one: an injury that should have cost a side its rating doesn't, and nobody rests when a group is settled. The fix is the one the league screen already has: one shared "play this matchday" function that both the live loop and the skip call, so the two can't drift apart. Checked by a verifier that plays the same seeds live and skipped and compares the stored inputs. *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 19 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-28 · Rest-player cut-offs, explained and what to do about them.**~~ Clubs rest players (`src/engine/rotation.ts`) only when their season can no longer change: they can't catch the leader, and they're mathematically safe or doomed on every line that matters. In the league screen those lines are two fixed numbers: a club counts as "still playing for Europe" if it can reach the top 5 (`LEAGUE_EURO_SPOTS`), and as "still in a relegation fight" if it can drop into the bottom 3 (`LEAGUE_RELEGATION_SPOTS`). The real zones (P3) now say something different per league and season. Seven places lead to Europe in most seasons, the Bundesliga has a play-off place above its two relegation places, and Ligue 1 relegated four in 2022/23. So today: *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

- **Europe race:** a 6th or 7th-placed side that can no longer reach the top 5 may rest players while it's still in a real Europa or Conference race.
- **Ligue 1 2022/23:** a side in 17th may rest players although it's going down.

What to do:

1. Read both cut-offs from `zonesFor(...)`: the qualify line becomes the last European place, and the drop line counts the play-off place too.
2. Keep the title rule as it is.
3. Measure before and after with a small verifier: how often clubs rest, the average points of the champion and of the relegated sides, and the player's win rate over a few thousand seasons.
4. Accept the change only if those numbers stay within noise, or move for a reason we can name.

This follows the "name the reason" rule and the lesson from The Dugout that the rating scale is a currency: rotation changes the strength of teams on the pitch, so it moves points. The same question applies to the Champions League (top 24) and World Cup (top 2) cut-offs, which are already correct for their formats. *(Done 22 September 2026 (batch 9): the two stakes lines come from the league's real zones for that season (`leagueCutoffs` in `rotation.ts`): the qualify line is the last European place, the drop line counts the play-off place; a league with no zones keeps 5 and 3. Measured by the new `verify-rotation` over 300 seasons across five leagues, played through `playFixture` with the same dice both ways: rested club-matches **5.29% → 4.91%** (fewer, for the named reason — more places are live), champion's points 88.61 → 88.69, relegated sides' 23.53 → 23.53, your win rate 40.61% → 40.56%. It also checks the two cases the entry names: a 7th-placed side out of the top five but in a real race for the last European place, and 17th in a four-down season.)*

~~**P8-29 · A custom scrollbar, everywhere.**~~ (Added 17 September 2026.) The platform's own scroll indicator is the one piece of another design system left on screen, and it turns up on every long list: the rivals list, the draft's player grid, the table, the press, the leagues browser, the bracket. Kit Drop needs its own: a square, hairline-thin track with a solid thumb in ink or cotton depending on the ground, drawn from the scroll position and fading out shortly after scrolling stops. It goes in `src/components/kit/` as one piece that wraps or decorates a `ScrollView` or `FlatList`, so a screen gets it by using the kit's scrolling container instead of a raw one. Constraints: the native indicator is hidden (`showsVerticalScrollIndicator={false}`), the thumb never handles touches on native (it's an indicator, not a control), on web it can be draggable because a mouse expects that, it respects reduced motion by not animating its fade, and it must not cost a frame on a 38-row table — the position comes from the scroll event, with no layout passes per row. *(Web version done 19 September 2026, in CSS (`webChrome.ts`): a 6px square solid thumb on a hairline track, cotton on nylon and ink on cotton. `KitScreen` writes the focused screen's ground to `<html data-ground>`. Chrome ignored the old webkit rules because the standard `scrollbar-color` was also set; that's now a Firefox-only fallback. The page and the long lists (draft, rivals, bracket, home, About, guide) show it on web. Not built: the native indicator, the fade after scrolling stops.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 19 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-30 · The same player can be drafted twice.**~~ (17 September 2026: "got Real Sociedad twice and a 4-4-2, and got two Oyarzabals up top".) A club-season can be spun more than once in a run, and nothing stops the same player being picked from both spins, so a shape can field two of the same footballer. The draft must treat a player as taken for the rest of the run: once picked, they're unavailable in any later squad list, marked as already yours rather than silently missing, and the same rule applies to the bench. `isPlayerAvailable` (`src/engine/draft.ts`) is the one place that decides this. Worth deciding with the maintainer: whether a *different season* of the same player (2019 Oyarzabal and 2024 Oyarzabal) counts as the same footballer. The rule reads best as "one of each footballer", which means keying on the player's identity rather than the player-season row. *(Done 19 September 2026: `footballerKey` (name + birth year, `src/engine/draft.ts`) marks every footballer already in the XI or on the bench as YOURS in later spins, in any season. The player id couldn't be the key: the full-path pool keeps its own `…_cucl` copy of each player.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

Your own eleven has to be drawn in your own formation on every route into the sheet: from the season screen, from the results list, from the match-day scrub and from a finished run. *(Done 22 September 2026 (batch 9): the league season already passed your formation; the gap was the Champions League's knockout legs. `koLegDetailRequest` takes `playerFormation`, and every route that builds a knockout leg passes it — the finals result page, the full path's result page, `openKoTie` (qualifying and knockout ties), the full path's final and the classic live knockouts. A leg opened from the match sheet's own tie card carries it too (P8-101's `requestFromContext`).)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 22 September 2026.)*

**Maintainer playtest notes after Phase 4's second pass (18 September 2026).** The pundits screen "looks okay". Everything else below is to fix. *(Done 22 September 2026 (batch 9): a live season pauses whenever its screen loses focus — a match sheet, a story, a club page, anything opened on top — and stays paused when you come back. ~~**P8-33 · Commentary needs far more to say.**~~ The commentary engine (`src/engine/commentary.ts`) reads only what the sheet stores as events: goals, penalties missed, cards, substitutions, injuries. FotMob's live feed covers much more of a match: shots saved, off target, blocked and off the woodwork; big chances missed; corners; free kicks in dangerous areas; offsides; fouls and their free kicks; VAR checks and overturned decisions; the added time announced; kick-off, half-time and full time with the score; and, in a shootout, every kick. The match sheet already generates most of this as per-player and per-team counts (shots, shots on target, big chances missed, offsides, corners, fouls). The work is to place those counts as timed, attributed events, seeded like everything else, so the feed has something to say every few minutes. First step: survey FotMob's (and SofaScore's) live event types properly and list which ones the sheet can already support. *(Done 22 September 2026 (batch 9), first slice: the chances between the events now have minutes and lines — every non-goal shot on the shot map (saved, off target, blocked, off the woodwork, with big chances called out), and every corner and offside the sheet counted. The shots are the shot map's own, from the same seed, so the feed and the map describe the same match; the minutes come from their own salted stream so they can never move a goal. About 23 lines a match, merged into the match sheet's commentary in minute order (kick-off, half-time and full time were already there). `verify-commentary` checks the count, the clock, the order and determinism over 2,000 matches. **Still to do:** fouls and their free kicks, VAR, the added time announced; and the Deep Match, which plays its own timeline with its own shot moments, so its feed needs the chances placed on *that* timeline, not these minutes.)* *(The maintainer, 22 September 2026: it's hard to tell your news from theirs, and the feed and the timeline look bland beside FotMob's (screenshots of FotMob's commentary and Facts timeline supplied). Make the comms look like FotMob's cards, and the timeline too. *(Done 22 September 2026, second slice. **More to say:** every foul, named from the sheet's own `foulsCommitted` and only while that player was on the pitch, with the free kick it gives (one in five "in a dangerous position"); the added time as the board goes up, placed before any stoppage-time event; and about one match in nine a goal **VAR rules out** (offside, handball or a foul in the build-up) — one of that side's own saved or wide shots told as it went, in the net then chalked off, so the shot map, the shot count and the score don't move; an offside call uses up one of the side's offsides. About 47 lines a match now. Shots and fouls are also kept inside the player's own minutes, which the first slice didn't do. **The Deep Match:** the same lines, placed on its own clock (`clockFromFrames`): a shot, corner, offside or foul lands on a minute where the live stats show that number moving. **FotMob's look:** the feed is cards and rows. A line with a heading (goal, card, change, injury, big chance, woodwork, VAR, added time) is a card — the minute in a badge, the heading, the player on his own row beside his club's crest, the words — with the side's club colour down its edge, so whose moment it is reads first; the rest are plain rows with a dot in the side's colour. The timeline has real icons (ball, card shapes, change arrows, injury, missed penalty, VAR), the score beside every goal, "+N minutes added" where the stoppage starts, each break as a pill with the score (HT 1 - 0), and a ruled-out goal on its side. `verify-commentary` now also checks the fouls and boards in the count, at most one VAR call, players inside their minutes, and that every Deep Match corner and foul lands on a minute its frames moved. **Still open:** the Deep Match only shows the latest line under the board; a full live feed there is the natural next step.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 22 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 22 September 2026: the Facts timeline is liked; a red card should carry red, as FotMob's does. *(Done 22 September 2026: a red card's card in the feed has FotMob's red wash across its top and a red minute badge.)*)*

The club has to be on the shirt (a short code, as in the rest of the app). In the honourable mentions, the YOURS tag repeats on row after row (P8-23's rule applies: one marker per region, and not one that repeats). *(Done 22 September 2026 (batch 9): each shirt on the awards' pitch shows the player's club as its short code (`clubCode`), under the surname. In the honourable mentions your players are marked by the row's background, not a YOURS tag on every row.)* *(The maintainer, 22 September 2026: to be replaced later by P8-125 (a player's nation and club behind him), and by the centralisation of the formation, so one formation drawing is used everywhere. **The first rule: if you create something, you use it again and again instead of creating another.** The awards' pitch, the draft's pitch, the match sheet's line-up and the verdict's pitch should become one component.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-35 · Awards arrive with no transition.**~~ Each new award just appears. It needs an entrance: at the very least a fade, ideally the stamp or settle `06-MOTION.md` gives a verdict-like moment. The same goes for most of the new screens: things that change or arrive should move per the motion document, not pop. *(Done 22 September 2026 (batch 9): each award on Awards Night now **stamps** in — it arrives at 115% and lands on `spring.stamp`, with its opacity cut in fast — instead of fading. It's a shared `stampIn` in `src/lib/motion.ts`, so the verdict-like moments elsewhere can use the same entrance. Reduced motion keeps it static. **Still open:** the entry's wider point, that most new screens should move per the motion document rather than pop.)* *(The maintainer, 22 September 2026: slow it down and make it more like a statement; it feels too fast. *(Done 22 September 2026: a heavier `verdictIn` in `src/lib/motion.ts`, built on the same `stampIn` (now parameterised): a 200 ms held breath, then the award comes down from 150% on a new `spring.verdict` (700 ms, no bounce, because a statement doesn't wobble) while it fades in over 260 ms. Each landing is a medium haptic on the phone (yours stays the success buzz), timed to the landing rather than the start. The beat is 3.4 s instead of 2.8, so the slower landing doesn't eat the reading time.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 22 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-36 · Tapping a player breaks the night, and the season view gives the game away.**~~ Several problems in one place: *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

- **Tapping a player leaves the screen.** From Awards Night or the verdict, tapping a player goes to the stats screen, and coming back lands on the result screen instead of where you were. Tapping has to open the player *on top of* the current screen and return to exactly the same place.
- **It's slow.** The stats screen regenerates every match of the run before showing anything ("loading time of doom and despair"). The player's season must come from the stats already computed, not a fresh pass.
- **It spoils the night.** The stats screen shows the award lists, so opening one player mid-ceremony reveals the winners still to come. The player view must show that player only.
- **Awards belong on the player.** Every award won on the night (Player of the Season, best under-21, golden boot and the rest) should show on that player's statistics as badges.
- **The numbers in the breakdown need explaining.** The column of points next to "Rating 7.2 over 30 games", "Goals" and the rest isn't explained, so it reads as noise. Label it ("award points") and say in one line what it is.
- **The defender award goes to full-backs.** Defender of the season mostly lands on LB and RB, even when a centre-back had the better defensive numbers, because the season score rewards assists and chances created that full-backs pile up. A defender's award should be decided on what makes a defender: tackles, interceptions, clearances, blocks, duels won, clean sheets, errors avoided, with attacking output counting for less. Model it on what the real game rewards, and check the result is plausible across positions (a centre-back should win it as often as the numbers say). *(Done 22 September 2026 (batch 10). Most of this already landed with Phase 5's player page: a player opens on top of Awards Night and back returns to the same beat (the night pauses), the page reads the run's cached numbers instead of regenerating the season, from the ceremony it shows that player only, and his honours are badges on his page. What was left: **the award points are labelled** — the breakdown under a winner has column heads (His season · Number · Award pts) and one line saying what award points are. **Defender of the season is decided on defending**: a `defenderScore` of tackles, interceptions, clearances, blocks, duels and clean sheets, minus errors and own goals, with goals and assists at a discount (weights in `DEF_WEIGHT`, `src/engine/stats.ts`), and the award shows that score's own breakdown. The new `verify-defender` plays 30 whole 20-club seasons through the real match sheet: centre-backs won the old award **7–10%** of the time and win the new one **100%**, the same as the pure defensive numbers (in this match engine a CB out-defends a full-back in every column: 2.0 tackles, 1.7 interceptions, 0.6 blocks per 90 against 1.6, 1.3, 0.2). If full-backs should sometimes win, that's the generator's per-position numbers to look at, not the award.)* *(The maintainer, 23 September 2026: the rest works. But a really great full-back season has to be able to win too — compared even with the great centre-backs — or have an award of its own; and no position should be shut out of every award. Split the midfielder award into attacking and defensive (a CM in both), add a winger award (RM, RW, LM, LW) and a full-back award. *(Done 23 September 2026. **Seven positional awards** now, and every position can win one: Golden glove (GK); Defender of the season (every defender); **Full-back of the season** (RB, LB, RWB, LWB — his defending plus what he gives going forward); **Defensive midfielder** (CDM and CM — tackles, interceptions, duels, passing, plus goals and assists); **Attacking midfielder** (CAM and CM); **Winger** (RW, LW, RM, LM); Forward (ST, CF). Golden boot, playmaker and man of the match stay open to everyone. Each award with its own score shows that score's breakdown. **Defender of the season, evened:** in raw numbers a centre-back out-defends a full-back every season, so for the ranking each full-back's defender score is lifted toward the centre-backs' by the ratio of the two groups' medians, to the power `DEF_EVEN` = 0.75. `verify-defender` (60 seasons, now fully seeded, so it's reproducible) measured it: centre-backs win it **75%** of seasons, full-backs 25% — when a full-back's season is exceptional for a full-back. The sweep: 0.6 → 97%, 0.7 → 83%, 0.8 → 65%, 0.9 → 52%. Across the 60 seasons GK, CB, RB, LB, CDM, CM, CAM, RW, LW and ST all won at least one award, which the script now checks. The script also found it wasn't deterministic — scorers were attributed with `Math.random` — and now seeds them.)*)* *(The maintainer, 23 September 2026: "we are missing a simple midfielder of the season award." *(Done 23 September 2026: Midfielder of the season is back, for every midfielder — holding, central, attacking and wide — on the season score, beside the defensive and attacking ones. `verify-awards` and `verify-defender` check it goes to a midfielder.)*)*

~~**P8-37 · Manager of the season needs rethinking, and real managers.**~~ Today it's "places beaten against the pundits, plus three for the title". Problems:

- **Improvement isn't linear.** 10th to 5th is a bigger achievement than 16th to 10th, even though it's fewer places, so place gains need weighting by where they happen.
- **It should reward how the team played,** not only the gap: the actual finish, points, how the side performed, and the best players it had.
- **The copy.** "Beat the pundits by the most places. Winning the title counts extra." says the wrong thing. The award isn't about the gap alone; the gap is one ingredient. The line should say what it really rewards.
- **Real managers.** Scrape each club-season's manager, so the award names a person, not a club. Your club's manager is you: your username. *(Done 22 September 2026 (batch 10), except the real managers. **The score:** each finish is valued on a curve (0 for last, 10 for first, quadratic), so climbing near the top is worth more — 10th to 5th now beats 16th to 10th. The award is the value gained over the pundits' tip, plus a quarter of where the side finished, plus 2 for the title, plus half a point for each of its players in the team of the season. **The copy:** "Who did most with what they had: beating the pundits, with places near the top worth more than places lower down, plus where they finished, the title, and the players they got the best out of." The headline also says how many of its players made the team of the season. **You are your club's manager:** when your side wins it, the award names your username, with the club under it. `verify-awards` checks the winner has the best manager score, that 10th→5th beats 16th→10th, and that your win names you. **Still open:** scraping each club-season's real manager — it needs a new source (Transfermarkt's club pages carry the coach) and a column in the clubs table.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 23 September 2026.)*

- **Half finished.** Below the new verdict the page is still the old one. It needs the full rebuild (tracked as Phase 4's result-screen work).
- **The pundits' table** almost works. It needs more colour: red for a club that did worse than tipped, gold for spot on, and more wow, such as a small sparkle on the exact calls.
- **The awards move to their own screen.** They make the result page far too long. The page gets a button, and the awards open on their own route.
- **Keep:** team of the matchday is "brilliant". Keep it as it is. *(Done 22 September 2026 (batch 10), the pundits' table: every call is coloured — better than tipped in volt, worse in misery red, and **spot on in gold with a sparkle** that twinkles now and then (staggered by row, still under reduced motion). The same in the cups' round table. Gold is a new token, `prim.gold`, for exact calls only. **Still open:** the result page's full rebuild below the verdict (Phase 4/5's result-screen work, and P8-54).)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 23 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 23 September 2026: the sparkle "makes it feel more alive, great call — make more like these". Recorded as a principle in P8-145.)*

Tapping the pinned player now replays the swing — picking a player up is its own mount of the tag, where before the pinned tag was reused and sat still.)* *(The maintainer, 23 September 2026: the pin still doubled — a drafted player kept his pin while another player was picked up and moved, so two showed until the next spin. *(Fixed 23 September 2026: picking any player up (a starter or a sub) takes the pin off the last pick, so the held player is the only one wearing it; the move then pins the moved player.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 23 September 2026.)*

A new shared `teamOfTheRound` (`src/engine/run-stats.ts`) regenerates only that round's sheets, with the same seed, rotation and absences as the match sheet, and picks the side with Awards Night's own `pickTeam`, so the team is the one the night will show. ~~**P8-44 · Ratings use SofaScore's colour scale.**~~ The maintainer supplied SofaScore's rating scale as the reference: https://files.sofascore.com/news/2024/10/03-Sofascore-News_Ratings-Scale-16_9-1024x576.png. The bands, as SofaScore publishes them and to be confirmed against that image: below 6.0 red, 6.0–6.4 orange, 6.5–6.9 yellow, 7.0–7.9 green, 8.0–8.9 teal, 9.0 and up blue. `ratingColor` in `src/theme.ts` moves to that scale, with the text on every band checked for contrast. **Also, as a note only:** study how SofaScore *computes* a player rating (the inputs, how position changes the weights, how a player's rating moves during a match) before touching the rating model in `match-detail.ts`. Nothing changes until that study is written up. *(Done 19 September 2026: `ratingColor` uses SofaScore's six bands, colours sampled from the reference image (#DC0C00, #ED7E07, #D9AF00, #00C424, #00ADC4, #374DF5). A new `ratingInk` gives the figure on each chip: cotton on red and blue, ink on the rest. Every chip uses it, and the match sheet's player table shows the chip when sorted by rating.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 19 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-45 · A proper settings menu.**~~ Not a couple of switches on You, but a settings screen of its own, with persistent preferences: reduced motion, haptics, the skip warning (P8-21), sound when there is any, and the light/dark question (P8-65). It has the two questions (skipping ahead, playing without a bench), **Less motion** — the app's own switch, on top of the phone's setting, applied through Reanimated's `ReducedMotionConfig` at the root and a shared `useReducedMotion` hook that every screen now reads — and **Haptics** (not on web, which has none). Every "turn it back on" line points to Settings. Not there yet, on purpose: sound (the app has none) and light or dark (P8-65 undecided); each gets a row when it exists.)* *(The maintainer, 23 September 2026: the screen looks nice; haptics didn't seem to work (none fired when picking a player from the club just drafted), and this isn't a game that needs them — don't implement haptics. *(Done 23 September 2026: haptics are out of the app — the Settings row, the preference, `src/lib/haptics.ts` and all 19 calls. `expo-haptics` is still in package.json, unused; removing it is a dependency change for the next native build.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 23 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-46 · Real icons instead of the text codes.**~~ GOAL, OG, YC, RC, SUB, INJ, POTM and the rest were a stopgap for emoji. They become proper icons from an icon library. The maintainer's suggestions are Arcticons, Icons8 or Flaticon. To check before choosing:

- **Arcticons** is a set of launcher app icons, not UI glyphs, so it may not have football symbols.
- **Icons8** and **Flaticon** need attribution or a paid licence for most sets, which matters for a public release and for the maturita licensing notes.
  Whatever is picked sits behind the kit's `Icon`, so it's one swap. *(Done 23 September 2026 (batch 11): every text code is now a mark, from one kit component, `EventMark` — goal, own goal (the ball in misery red), assist, yellow and red cards (drawn as cards, in a new referee-yellow token and misery red), on and off (arrows in volt and red), injury, penalty scored and missed, VAR and player of the match (a gold star). A count above one is written beside the mark (a hat-trick is the ball and a 3). It replaced GOAL/OG/YC/RC/SUB/INJ/POTM/IN/MISS/▲/▼ in the match timeline, the player rows, the lineup pitch and its bench, the player page's match rows, the live match feed and the shootout. **The library:** Ionicons, which the kit's `Icon` already uses — MIT-licensed, no attribution, so it's clean for a public release and the maturita licensing notes; Arcticons is launcher icons (no football glyphs), and Icons8/Flaticon need attribution or a licence. The cards are drawn rather than taken from a set, because a card is a shape.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 24 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-47 · The shot map, reworked.**~~ The shot map is "too condensed and hard to see". Instead: *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

- **Greyed out by default.** All shots start greyed out.
- **Step through them.** Arrows above the map go shot by shot in the order they were taken (by minute). The map itself isn't tapped.
- **Highlight the current shot** with its taker, minute, outcome and xG.
- **A shot route:** a simple line from where the shot was struck to where it ended (in the goal, saved, wide, off the woodwork), so the map feels alive.
  The heat map and average positions are "nice" and stay. *(Done 23 September 2026 (batch 11): all shots start greyed out; arrows above the map step through them in the order they were taken, and the current one lights up in its outcome's colour with its taker, minute, outcome and xG ("23' · Kubo · Saved · 0.12 xG · shot 4 of 11"). A line draws out from where it was struck to where it ended — in the net, at the keeper, wide, charged down (dashed), off a post — with the ball running along it (still under Less motion). Every shot's minute comes from one place, `timedShots` (`src/engine/commentary.ts`): non-goal shots take the minute the commentary told them at, goals and penalties their event's, so the map and the feed agree; where each ended is seeded. `verify-commentary` checks every shot has a minute, the order, that each ends on the pitch, and determinism. The heat map and average positions are unchanged.)* *(The maintainer, 24 September 2026: brilliant — but the first shot should already be picked, and on every step the route flashed in the top-left corner for a split second. *(Fixed 24 September 2026: the map opens on the first shot; the route's line and ball are drawn from the boot on their first frame, where before the frame before the animation landed drew them from the pitch's corner (0, 0).)*)* *(The maintainer, 24 September 2026: for a split second every other shot's route snapped to where the first one ended. *(Fixed 24 September 2026: the route was animated by one progress value that lived in the map and was reset only after the render, so each step's first frame drew the new route at full length — at the previous shot's end — before it snapped back and grew. Each shot's route is now its own component, mounted fresh per shot, starting at zero with its own coordinates, so it has no previous shot to remember.)*)*

**P8-52 · A domestic cup and last season's winners for the full Champions League path.** The full path has no FA Cup, and a domestic cup matters to European places. It can be done, but it pulls in more:

- **Last season's winners.** The previous season's Champions League, Europa League and Conference League winners need to be known for both seasons in the data, because they take places in the access list.
- **A ceremony before the run.** The cups and those winners are shown before it starts, with the same rule as every ceremony: it can be skipped to the end.
  This is a pass of its own, with data work first.
  *(Done 28 September 2026 (batch 24), as the full path through all three competitions; the research is [`docs/europe/05-THE-FULL-PATH.md`](../europe/05-THE-FULL-PATH.md). **Last season's winners** are data now (`EURO_HOLDERS`, from UEFA's archived 2026–27 access lists): Paris Saint-Germain (Champions League), Aston Villa (Europa League, into the Champions League) and Crystal Palace (Conference League, into the Europa League). **Every association's cup** is played (P8-173's engine), and the cup winners take their places. **The Europa and Conference Leagues' access lists** are in, one club one competition, a place passing down its league's table when its club is already in (a cup winner already in passes his down too). **The three qualifying ladders** run round by round, each fed the losers of the one above: Champions League Q1 losers into the Conference League, the rest into the Europa League, the Europa League's into the Conference League. You play whichever you end up in: its league phase (six pots and six games for the Conference League), its knockouts, its names, and the tier and score of that competition (`uel_qf_exit`, weighted 0.8 and 0.65 as in the classic modes); going out in the Conference League's qualifying is the only real exit. **The ceremony** is the rest-of-Europe screen, skippable as before: the champions, then the holders and where they play, then every cup winner. The league tables mark Europa and Conference League places too. `verify-europe-path` plays 318 seasons on the real 53 associations: every league phase 36, nobody in two competitions or twice in a round, every Champions League qualifying loser dropping, the holders placed right, every route adding up, each field drawing under the full rules, every tier scored and named. **To apply:** redeploy `submit-run` (the score ladder).)*

Do it after P8-71's centralisation has given tables, ties and scorelines one component each, or the rebuild copies them a fifth time.)* *(24 September 2026: the P8-71 research makes this batch step 6 of its plan; [07-PHASE-TWO-PLAN step 6](../centralisation/07-PHASE-TWO-PLAN.md) lists the items it absorbs: round-by-round lookup and team of the matchday for every mode, highlights, the bracket, history on the same screen, one run-data source.)* *(Done 3 October 2026 (centralisation step 6 / Wave F): the four result screens are one, `app/game/result.tsx` routing to a family per mode, all drawn by `ResultShell`; the tables, bracket, pundits, cup, Europe and squad live in the run hub's tabs, one tap from a door. ~~**P8-63 · Live rounds, everywhere they play out.**~~ *(Widened 20 September 2026: this was written for the Champions League knockouts, but the maintainer wants the same behaviour in every live phase — the domestic season, qualifying, the league phase, the World Cup group stage and both brackets and even the deep match, for example the pause on big moments.)*

- **Newest round first.** A new round should appear above the previous one, not below it. That also fixes having to scroll down to follow the run.
- **Follow the live one, and a way back up.** The page keeps the round being played in view as it builds, and a "to the top" control returns to the newest round after you've scrolled back through the earlier ones.
- **Wait when you can't see it.** If your live match is out of view (you scrolled away without pausing, or opened a match), it should stop on its own and wait until you're back.
- **More varied commentary.** The per-match commentary is bland (see P8-33).
- **Pause on the big moments.** The clock stops briefly on commentary moments: goals, fouls, cards. *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 12): in the live knockouts (the Champions League, the World Cup and the full path) the **newest round sits on top**, where you already are; the page returns to it as each round opens; scroll down through earlier rounds and a **Back to the newest round** tag appears, and while you're away your live match and the next round **wait for you**. The live match **holds for a beat** on a goal or a red card, and the Deep Match's clock holds on a goal, a red or a missed penalty. Already done earlier: the season pausing when you look away (P8-32), the richer commentary (P8-33). **For P8-71:** the full path's knockout view is a near-copy of the classic one; they should become one.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 24 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-66 · The graphs have no legend.**~~ The club page's position graph and the player page's match-by-match rating trend have no axes, labels or legend, so they don't say anything on their own. The position graph (1st, middle, last) and the player's rating trend (6.0, 7.0, 8.0, with rating-coloured points) both use it.)* *(Reopened 19 September 2026, the maintainer's spec: *(Done to the spec, 19 September 2026: *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 19 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-67 · The run hub needs a design pass, and has to reuse what already exists.**~~ It's bland, and it's crowded: the tabs sat against each other (the spacing is fixed; the rest isn't), and the sections are close together. Where the app already has a piece for something (the season strip, the position graph, the table with zone tapes, result rows), the hub uses that exact piece rather than a lookalike, because near-copies make it feel pieced together. *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 12), the reuse half: the hub's own match rows became the season's `ResultRow` (crests, scorers, your side marked as everywhere), its press rows the season's `StoryItem`, the squad's "8G 3A · 2 MOTM" the match marks (P8-46), and the sections have room (they sat 8 px apart). **Still open:** its flavour (P8-79).)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 24 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-68 · AI clubs change formation between matchdays.**~~ Other clubs should seem to adapt: a formation per club per match, chosen for a reason (form, opponent, injuries), stored on the match like scorers (attribute once), and shown on the match sheet. *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 12): each club has a repertoire — its usual shape and two others — and picks one per match for a reason the match already stores: the squad it has that day (absences change which shape its players fit) and the opponent (clearly stronger → more defenders, clearly weaker → more attackers); a rotated side keeps its usual shape. The shape is chosen inside the matchday squad the club already picked, and only the eleven is re-drawn from it, so no stored scorer can fall outside it — runs saved before this still open correctly (at worst a scorer starts on the bench and comes on before his goal). The match sheet's lineup shows the shape played. The new `verify-shapes` checks all of that and measures it: clubs change shape in 21% of matches, 74% of the changes against a much stronger side add defenders and 83% against a much weaker one add attackers (at the first weight the lean didn't show; tuned to `LEAN_PER` = 1). Every engine verifier still passes. **Not shown yet:** the reason for a change on the sheet.)* *(The maintainer, 24 September 2026: couldn't see AI sides change shape against each other. *(Found and fixed 24 September 2026. Measured on three real leagues' squads (LaLiga 18/19, Premier League 22/23, Serie A 20/21), counting matches away from each club's most-played shape: without a new reason, 12 of 20 Premier League clubs played one shape all season — with no injuries and an even opponent, the same squad fits the same shape best every week, and the first measure (changes from the club's hashed shape) hid it by counting a club that always switched to the same alternative as "changing" every match. Added the reason managers change most: a seeded **manager's call** for this opponent, this week (`TINKER` = 1). Now a club is off its usual shape in about 23% of matches in all three leagues, only 3-5 clubs per league never change, and `verify-shapes` confirms the opponent lean still shows through (63% of changes against a much stronger side add defenders, 65% against a much weaker one add attackers). Every engine verifier passes.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-69 · The shared story needs to look like the app.**~~ The shared image is too zoomed in and too small, and nothing marks it as Perfection or Misery. Give it a proper frame at a bigger size, with the app's name and the matchday on it, like the run label (which is praised as it is). *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 13): a card made for sharing, instead of a crop of the page. Built like the run label: a riveted label on the kit's offset shadow, the run's colourway tape down its edge (the league's own, P8-55), the matchday, the headline as a super, the standfirst, the table as it stood with crests and points, and the **Perfection or Misery** wordmark under it. It's laid out at 360 points off-screen and captured at 1080 pixels wide, so the image is big and sharp. **To check on a phone:** some Android versions capture an off-screen view as blank; if the shared image is empty, the card moves on-screen behind the page.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-70 · The club page shows its best XI and formation.**~~ A pitch with the club's most-used eleven in its most-used formation (the awards' `FormationPitch`), and on the MATCHES tab each match shows the formation that club played. *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 13): the club page opens with its **most-used XI** — the eleven that started most, in the shape it played most ("23 of 38 matches"), on the shared pitch, each shirt with his starts and his rating square, and the next in line under it. On the Matches tab every match wears the shape the club played in it. The shapes are read off each match's regenerated sheet when the run's stats are computed and kept on the match (attribute once), so the page never regenerates anything. Runs that don't keep match-by-match detail don't show it.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

**P8-71 · Centralisation, across the whole app.** Go through every screen, find the things that mean the same (score rows, player rows, club rows, rating chips, section heads, graphs), and give each one component. It extends P8-49: the app should feel like one thing, not pieces put together. *(The maintainer, 24 September 2026: stop before this item; it becomes **two phases** — first research: find everything that is drawn or computed differently even though it should be the same; then make all the changes. Found while working on batch 13, for the research list: the live knockout view exists twice (classic and full path, P8-63); the two-leg live periods existed twice and one copy had a bug the other had fixed (leg 2 skipping extra time); club colours were read two ways (the match sheet's lookup vs the raw `clubs` row); tie view models are built in five places (`KoTieVM` lost the crests); the old match sheet still puts flags inside text (`withFlag`).)* *(Phase one done 24 September 2026: the research is in [`docs/centralisation/`](../centralisation/00-README.md) — 72 items across feature parity (F), drawing (C), computing (L), marks and colours (A) and two screen-only findings (S), each with evidence and the shared base it should come from, walked screen by screen, and an eight-step phase-two plan with Build / Done when steps. Headlines: nation flags break because `ClubName` and `ResultRow` default to a crest and the pundits' tables draw no mark at all (one `TeamMark` by id fixes it); the classic and full-path result screens print "21th" and every `surname` copy turns "van Dijk" into "Dijk"; the World Cup's matchday lookback is state nothing sets; the full path has drifted furthest. Step 6 of the plan is P8-54. Seven open decisions (D1–D7) have defaults in the README.)*

The old sections are the topics, brought up to date where the app moved on: the awards (every position, the team of the season, the manager), the pundits and the press are new topics, the knockouts mention the finished bracket, and the ▲/▼, ★ and "Full stats →" of the old copy are gone. ~~**P8-73 · About page and a real version button.**~~

- **Quick Sim Tester:** unlocked by tapping the "Made in Slovakia" line instead of the version number. It stays at the bottom of About.
- **Version button:** the version becomes a real button. It shows on the main screen and in every menu before a game starts, and it opens a version history.
- **Version history:** each version with its date and what changed, pieced together from git history (GitHub), the docs and their dates. Where a date is inferred, say so.
- **Known dev noise:** on Android dev builds, LogBox sometimes shows "configured linking in multiple places" after a reload. The app has one navigator, so it's from the reload. Check it on a cold start; if it shows there, find the cause. *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 14): **the tester** is behind eight taps on "Made in Slovakia" (dev builds only, as before) and stays at the bottom of About. **The version** is a real button, `VersionButton` ("V0.8.0 ›"), on Home, You, About and every menu before a run starts (mode, difficulty, custom difficulty, shape), and it opens **`/versions`**, the history. The history comes from the GitHub commits (hoopjakub/perfection-or-misery: 37 commits, 8 June to 19 September, read 24 September), with the day's documents filling in what a short commit message doesn't say, and the roadmap for the work since the last commit: eight versions, 0.1 (12 June, the commit that says "probably the first v0.1") to 0.8 (Phases 7 and 8, not committed yet), each saying where it comes from. The app said 0.0.1 until now, so the versions are numbered after the fact and `app.json`/`package.json` are **0.8.0** to match. **Dev noise:** the app has one navigator and no linking of its own (only `scheme: pom`), so the "configured linking in multiple places" warning is the reload remounting the root; worth a look on a cold start.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-74 · The palette, and the win/loss decision.**~~ The colours in the app today (`src/theme.ts`, `prim`): *(Partly decided 19 September 2026: misery red is in (`#FF2E4D` fill and text on nylon, `#D1123F` text on cotton), first used by Delete account. Using it for losses everywhere is still to do.)* *(Losses done 19 September 2026: the loss Tag is misery red with ink text and keeps its striped edge, which covers every W/D/L result tag, DOWN n, the blunder and the gap tags. The season strip's L cell is filled red like W is volt. The OUT stamp has a red edge, a knocked-out club tag has a red edge, the qualifying ladder's knocked-out colour is red, and the match sheet's form pills use volt/red/grey instead of the old yellow and danger red. Left as the stripe: the Misery verdict, table OUT zones, disabled and warning states.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 19 September 2026.)*

| Token                | Hex                       | Meaning                                       |
| -------------------- | ------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| Nylon                | `#141416`               | Black background for live play and Misery     |
| Nylon raised         | `#1F1F22`               | Cards on black                                |
| Nylon sunken         | `#0B0B0C`               | Pressed areas on black                        |
| Rule (on black)      | `#34343A`               | Hairlines on black                            |
| Cotton               | `#F3F3F0`               | Off-white background for setup and reading    |
| Label                | `#E2E2DE`               | Sunken areas on white                         |
| Rule (on white)      | `#CFCFCA`               | Hairlines on white                            |
| Ink                  | `#0C0C0D`               | Text on white, and on any volt or orange fill |
| Ink muted / faint    | `#5A5A60` / `#8A8A90` | Secondary text on white                       |
| Cotton muted / faint | `#A4A4AB` / `#6C6C73` | Secondary text on black                       |
| Safety orange        | `#FF5A00`               | Always means you (the zip-tie tag)            |
| Boot volt            | `#D5FF3F`               | Perfection, and wins                          |
| Draw grey            | `#6E6E74`               | Draws                                         |

- **Mode colours**, which say where you are and never good or bad: World Cup `#3CAC3B` `#2A398D` `#E61D25`, UCL `#2F4BFF`, Chaos `#C8261B`, Cursed `#7234F0`.
- **Fonts:** Barlow Condensed Black Italic for headlines, Martian Mono for tags, Archivo for body text.
- **Today:** a win is volt, a loss is the black-and-white hazard stripe, a draw is grey. There's no red anywhere, on purpose: the stripe reads without colour, and red would sit next to orange (you) and the Chaos colour.
- **The maintainer's logo drafts** use pure RGB green (`#00FF2x`) and pure red (`#FF0000`). Neither is in the system, and both are the harshest possible versions.
- **Recommendation, still to be decided:**
  - Win / Perfection stays volt `#D5FF3F`, which also replaces the logo's green.
  - Loss / Misery keeps the hazard stripe as the main mark, plus one "misery red" `#FF2E4D` for small places a stripe can't fit: tiny chips and the logo's bottom half. It leans pink, away from orange and from the brick-red Chaos colour, so "you" and "lost" never look alike, and ink text reads on it.
  - Draw stays grey `#6E6E74`.
  - The alternative keeps the app free of red: the logo's misery half is filled with the hazard stripe. That's more on-brand but weaker as an app icon.
- **When the maintainer picks,** add the token to `prim`/`ROLES` and use it for loss everywhere a loss is marked.

~~**P8-79 · The run hub has no flavour, and the bracket needs to be a real bracket.**~~

- It looks right, but it's plain: no country flags, no tags for who survived or went out, and no third-place play-off.

The ties are put in bracket order by where each winner turns up next, so the lines are true. It keeps the preview's pinch-zoom-and-pan canvas (moved, not copied), and the World Cup's **third-place play-off** hangs under the final. It's used by the bracket preview, the run hub's Bracket tab and all three cup result screens, which drop the old list (`KnockoutRoundsView` is gone; its row stays for qualifying). **Flavour:** flags everywhere in the hub — a new kit `TeamMark` decides crest or flag from the team's id, and `ClubName`, `ResultRow` and the table rows use it, so a nation can't be drawn as an initials badge (this also fixes the pundits table and the stories, docs/centralisation A-02/A-03); a group's third place says IN or OUT once the run is over (it said "into the race"); and every club's row carries how far it got (R16, QF, SF, FINAL, WON, 3RD, 4TH). **Found on the way:** the hub decided who won a World Cup semi-final by who played in a later round, and both semi-finalists play in the third-place game, so side A always "won"; the third-place game is now left out of that check.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-82 · The UCL league phase's infinite strip, and why it keeps happening.**~~ The Champions League league phase has an element that stretches down forever again, like the Chaos label (P8-02) and the World Cup red card (P8-62). There have been many of these. Investigate the common cause: it's probably an unbounded ScrollView, or a flex child with no height inside a scroll. Fix it once, where every caller routes through, and add a check so it can't come back. *(Root cause found and fixed 19 September 2026: the kit's `Stripe` drew its SVG at `height="100%"` inside boxes whose height comes from their row. On Android that's a layout loop: the SVG asks for the parent's height, the parent grows to fit it, and it never settles. Every "infinite strip" (the sign-in notice, Chaos label, World Cup red card, UCL OUT rows) is a `Stripe`. The SVG is now absolutely positioned, so it fills the box and never sizes it. Still to confirm on device.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-85 · Ranks: find yourself, filter, and a weekly board.**~~

- **You:** your runs carry the YOU tag or an orange outline, and your own place shows even when you're outside the list ("You: 214th").
- **Filters:**
  - by mode: All Time, League, Chaos, Cursed, and each tournament (Champions League, the full path, World Cup), plus "all tournaments";
  - by difficulty: easy, medium, hard, or custom with a hardness range (e.g. 6.0–11).
- **Two boards:** all time, and this week.
  - The week runs Monday 00:00 to Sunday 23:59: a run at 23:59 on Sunday counts for the old week, and one at 00:01 on Monday starts the new one.
  - Nothing is deleted. The weekly board is the same `runs` table filtered by `created_at` from the last Monday 00:00.
  - Do it in the query or as a database view, not a scheduled job, so there's nothing to "reset".
  - Decide the time zone once (Slovakia, Europe/Bratislava, is the obvious choice) and say it on the screen. *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 14): **two boards**, All time and This week; the week is Monday 00:00 to Sunday 23:59 Slovak time and the screen says so. It's the same `runs` table filtered from the week's start, worked out in the query (`src/lib/week.ts`, with the summer-time switch; `scripts/verify-week.ts` checks the edges and two years of weeks), so nothing resets. **Filters:** mode (All Time, League, Chaos, Cursed, each tournament, and all tournaments) and difficulty (Easy, Medium, Hard, or Custom with a hardness range from 3.0–11 to 10.0–11). **You:** your runs carry the YOU tag and an orange outline, and your own place shows above the list even outside the fifty ("YOU 214th · outside the fifty"). A hardness range filters the best 1,000 custom runs on the phone, because the hardness sits inside JSON (noted in the code, with the fix if custom runs outgrow it).)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-86 · Tournament icons are missing.**~~ The tournament modes have no icon where the other modes do. Give each one (Champions League, the full path, World Cup) its icon from the P8-46 icon set. *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 14): every mode card wears an icon, the tournaments included, from the kit's icon set (Ionicons, P8-46): All Time ∞, League a ball, Chaos a flash, Cursed a skull, the Champions League Finals a star and the Full path a signpost (the two share a competition, so the icon is what tells them apart), the World Cup a globe. The tournaments keep their competition mark (P8-12) beside the icon.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-87 · Delete account should be red.**~~ The Delete account row takes the loss/Misery colour decided in P8-74 (misery red `#FF2E4D`, if that's the pick), so it reads as dangerous at a glance. *(Done 19 September 2026: `danger` rows are misery red (edge, icon, label), from the new `prim.misery` / `roles.loss` / `roles.lossText` tokens.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 19 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-88 · A real profile page, one the player shapes.**~~ Today "You" is a name tag and a few rows. It becomes a proper profile, and each player chooses what others see.

- **Identity:** name; profile picture, compressed on the device before upload (a must: resize to about 512px and re-encode, never the raw camera photo); real-life favourite team, shown as a small badge beside the name everywhere the name appears; favourite player.
- **Record:** world rank, best tier, chosen runs pinned to the profile, chosen achievements on display, total playing time, runs played.
- **Look:** a backdrop colour and effect for the profile. It's stored in its own table (colour, effect, and room for more later), so it can grow without touching `profiles`. It stays inside the palette (P8-74): the effects are Kit Drop trims (tape, stripe, rivets, stitched edge), not glows or gradients.
- **Privacy:** a visible-to-others switch per section (runs, achievements, playing time, favourites), all readable only through RLS that honours those switches.
- **Storage:** Supabase Storage, one avatars bucket; you can only write your own file, and everyone can read. Deleting the account (Phase 6's `delete-account`) must also remove the avatar and the look row. *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 14), **to apply:** the database part is written, not applied — run `supabase/profile.sql` once in the Supabase SQL editor. Until then the app shows the plain profile and the edit screen says it needs the update. **The page:** `/u/<id>`, anyone's profile: the look (a band in the chosen palette colour with a Kit Drop trim: tape, stripe, rivets or a stitched edge), picture, name and favourite team's badge, the record (world rank, best tier, runs played, playing time), favourites, up to three pinned runs and three trophies on display. **Editing:** `/profile-edit`, from You. **Privacy:** a switch per section (pinned runs, trophies, playing time, favourites). Because `profiles` is public-readable for Ranks and row security can't hide single columns, the private fields live in `profile_details` (owner-only), and everyone else reads them through the database function `public_profile()`, which returns a hidden section as nothing; the badge by your name follows the favourites switch. **The look** has its own table, `profile_looks`. **Playing time:** each run now saves its length (`duration_seconds`, from choosing the mode to the save, capped at four hours). **Avatars:** one public `avatars` bucket, each player writing only their own folder; the picture is shrunk to 512 px and re-encoded as JPEG before upload. On the web that works now; on a phone it needs an image picker (`expo-image-picker` and `expo-image-manipulator`), which means installing them and a new dev build — **waiting for the maintainer's go-ahead**. **Deleting the account** removes the avatar and both new rows (`delete-account`). With server scoring on, `duration_seconds` also has to be added to `submit-run`'s allowed columns after the SQL is applied (the SQL file says so).)* *(25 September 2026: the maintainer ran `supabase/profile.sql`. Picking a picture on the phone is built: the system photo picker with a square crop, then `expo-image-manipulator` shrinks it to 512 px and saves a JPEG, uploaded as an ArrayBuffer (a Blob read from a file:// URI can upload empty on Android). `expo-image-picker` 17 and `expo-image-manipulator` 14 are installed with `npx expo install`, and the picker's config plugin is in `app.json` with the camera and microphone permissions turned off (only the library is used). A new Android development build is queued on EAS, since both are native modules. `submit-run` now allows `duration_seconds`; it needs redeploying.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 25 September 2026: You still showed only the name tag; others' pictures didn't show; the pinned-runs and trophies lists could grow forever and weren't sortable. *(Fixed 25 September 2026: You shows your picture beside the tag, and refreshes after a save or an upload. A phone upload now reads the file through `expo-file-system` (a `fetch(file://)` into an ArrayBuffer isn't dependable in React Native and could upload an empty file nobody could see) and refuses an empty one. Pictures uploaded before this fix may be empty: upload again. The pinned runs are the same garment labels as everywhere, a page of eight at a time with Show more, sorted Newest or Best score, the pinned ones first; the trophies page the same way. The Republic of Ireland pundit's flag was missing (the country table only knew "Rep. Ireland"): fixed.)*)* *(The maintainer, 25 September 2026: time played doesn't work. *(The cause: server scoring is on (`EXPO_PUBLIC_SERVER_SCORING=1`), so runs are saved by the `submit-run` edge function, and the deployed one still drops `duration_seconds` until it's redeployed (`npx supabase functions deploy submit-run`); the one run on the account was also saved before the column existed. From 25 September 2026 the profile shows Played only once there's time recorded, instead of "0 min".)*)* *(The fixes above (pictures, pinned runs and trophies, the Ireland flag): Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026. *(The maintainer, 25 September 2026: profile pictures can't be seen on the web. *(The cause: the web on Vercel is still the 19 September deploy. Its bundle has none of the profile code (no avatars, no public profile, no profile editing), while the pictures themselves are served fine (the avatars bucket answers 200 with an image and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`). They show once the current code is committed and the web is deployed again.)*)*)* *(The maintainer, 25 September 2026: the pictures don't show on the local web either (an empty grey box where the picture should be). *(Fixed 25 September 2026: the cause was the dev server, not the app. `metro.config.js` sent `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp` (for the web SQLite's SharedArrayBuffer), and that blocks every cross-origin image that doesn't send a `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` header. Supabase Storage doesn't send one, so the browser refused the pictures, and the box with no initials was the tell (a missing picture shows initials). It sends `credentialless` now, which loads them without cookies and keeps the page cross-origin isolated in Chrome, Edge and Firefox; `docs/Web & Desktop Deployment.md` says the same for when production needs the header. **Restart the web dev server** for it to take effect.)*)*

- **Sharing:** sharing someone else's run (the run label, a story, awards) carries their name and badge on the shared card, so it's clear whose run it was. Your own share says it's yours. *(Done 24 September 2026 (batch 14): a run's page says whose it is — the verdict on every result screen and the run hub show the owner (picture, name, team badge, "YOUR RUN" or "RUN BY"), a tap from their profile, and Ranks shows each run's player the same way. The shared image carries it too ("YOUR RUN" or "TOM'S RUN" on the verdict card) and so does the shared text ("My run: …" / "Tom's run: …"). Stories and Awards Night can't be shared from someone else's run (a saved run keeps no press, and Awards Night is the live run's), so the verdict card is where it matters.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-90 · Friends: requests and a friends list, with the UI they never had.**~~

- **What exists:** `src/lib/friends.ts` and `src/lib/versus.ts` have the logic (send, accept, list, versus challenges), but no screen reaches them, so friends can't be used or tested at all.
- **To build:** find a player by username; send, accept and decline requests; a friends list on the profile with each friend's latest run and rank; notifications for requests.
- **The database:** `friendships` and `notifications` have no INSERT policy while `friends.ts` inserts into both on accept. The needed rules are written, not applied, at the bottom of `supabase/policies.sql`. Apply them with this work, and make `friends.ts` check its errors instead of ignoring them.
  *Added 19 September 2026, with the batch 2 playtest:* *(Done 25 September 2026 (batch 15), **to apply:** run `supabase/friends.sql` once in the Supabase SQL editor. **Why not the insert policies policies.sql suggested:** an open insert on `friendships` lets any signed-in player write a friendship with anyone, no request needed, and one on `notifications` lets anyone post into anyone's notifications. Instead three checked database functions do everything: `send_friend_request` (asking someone who already asked you accepts them), `respond_friend_request` (accept or decline) and `remove_friend`; they write the friendship rows and the notifications themselves, and the tables stay closed to direct writes. Guests can't make friends. `src/lib/friends.ts` is rewritten on them and throws every error (the old version ignored them, so accepting failed silently). **The screen:** `/friends`, from You: what's new (requests and accepted requests), requests to answer, find a player by username (as you type), your friends each with their world rank and latest run, and the requests you're waiting on. **On a profile:** Add friend / Request sent / Accept / Friends (with Remove, confirmed). **Notifications:** a count on the You tab (orange, it's yours to deal with), refreshed when the app comes back to the front; opening Friends marks them read.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)*

- **The button:** during a knockout round, a button opens the bracket.
- **Pausing:** opening it pauses the playing match, since you're leaving the live screen.
- **What it shows:** the whole bracket, with finished ties and their results, and every other tie in the round that's still being played, with its live score as it stands. *(Done 25 September 2026 (batch 15): every knockout (classic, World Cup, full path) has **See the bracket** at the top. It opens the shared bracket (P8-79) on its own page, and **your live match pauses** while you're there — `LiveMatch` now holds whenever its screen isn't in front (a match sheet too), and carries on when you come back. Finished rounds show their results; the round being played shows **every tie as it stands at the same moment as yours**: the live match reports how far through the tie it is, and each other tie is read at that same point from its stored goal minutes (two legs share the time half and half, extra time counts at the end), so the bracket never shows a result your match hasn't reached. Rounds still to come show `?` and the day they're played (P8-92). `scripts/verify-live-bracket.ts` checks that a tie read this way starts at 0–0, never goes backwards, is leg 1's result halfway, and ends on the full result.)* *(The maintainer, 25 September 2026: See the bracket looked out of place; and during a live knockout, a match sheet opened mid-round let you tap a tie still being played and see its final score. *(Fixed 25 September 2026: See the bracket is a proper secondary plate. A match sheet's timeline no longer includes the round being played until your match in it is over — the same rule for the classic and World Cup knockouts.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 26 September 2026: See the bracket didn't stop the knockouts once you were out. *(Fixed 26 September 2026: the rounds reveal on their own timers when you have no live match, and they held while you'd scrolled away from the top (P8-63) but not while another screen was on top. They wait for the screen to be in front now, in every knockout (classic, World Cup, full path), and so do the full path's Europe's seasons and qualifying reveals; they carry on when you come back. The playing loops already stopped on leaving (P8-32), and a live match already paused (P8-91); every other timer in the simulation screens was checked and is one of those or cosmetic.)*)*

~~**P8-92 · Dates and kick-off times.**~~ Every match gets a date and a kick-off time, and rounds are spread over several days, as real competitions are.

- **Why:** it makes the game feel alive, and P8-91's bracket needs it to say what's live, what's finished and what's still to come.
- **Where it shows:** fixtures, results, the match sheet's header and the season strip.
- **How:** the schedule is fixed when the draw is made and stored with the run (attribute once, store on the match), like the scorers, so every view agrees. *(Done 25 September 2026 (batch 15): `src/engine/schedule.ts` gives every match a date and a kick-off time, in each competition's real rhythm: a league weekly from mid-August with the international breaks free, extra midweek rounds at Christmas when there are more matchdays than weekends, and a round spread Friday to Monday at the usual times; the Champions League on Tuesdays and Wednesdays (the league phase September to January, its last matchday all on one Wednesday, the knockouts February to May, the final on the last Saturday of May — 31 May 2025 and 30 May 2026, as they really were; the full path's qualifying in July and August); the World Cup in its real window, 11 June to 19 July 2026, the final on the 19th. **Stored, in effect:** the kick-off is worked out from what every match already stores (its round, its season, its two sides), so the live screen, the match sheet, the run hub and a run saved months ago all agree, and older saved runs get dates too — the two sides, not the seed, decide the day and the time, so a fixture listed before it's played keeps its kick-off once it has a seed. **Where it shows:** the match sheet's header, your fixtures (Champions League, World Cup, full path), your result card on every live screen, and the matchday picked on the league result's strip. `scripts/verify-schedule.ts` checks the order of every round, the real finals' dates, the World Cup windows and that the same match always gets the same kick-off.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)*

- **World Cup:** about 15 venues. Which hosted group games and which hosted the knockouts and the final are recorded, and a map shows them. *(Done 25 September 2026 (batch 15): **club grounds:** `scripts/scrape-stadiums.ts` read every club's stadium page on Transfermarkt (753 of 766 of the seeds' clubs; resumable, paced, a timeout on every request) into `scripts/seed/stadiums.json` and the generated `src/data/stadiums.ts` (753 club names; one real club, several ids). It's each club's **current** ground, so an older season can show a club's newer stadium (Tottenham before 2019). **Where a match was played:** `src/data/venues.ts` — the home side's ground; a Champions League final at that edition's venue (Munich's Allianz Arena for 2024/25, Budapest's Puskás Aréna for 2025/26); the World Cup at its **sixteen** stadiums (not fifteen), with the real knockout plan: the final at MetLife, the semi-finals in Dallas and Atlanta, the quarter-finals in Boston, Los Angeles, Miami and Kansas City, third place in Miami. A group plays around one region as the real ones do, but which group plays where is the game's own, since the game's draw isn't the real draw. The ground shows under the date on the match sheet. **The map:** the World Cup result has a Grounds section — the three host countries drawn from the globe's own outlines, every stadium on it (the final's in gold), the ones your run played at ringed in orange, and each ground's city, capacity and the rounds it hosts.)* *(The maintainer, 25 September 2026: the Grounds are a great addition; the grounds you played at should be filled orange instead of ringed, and the rows squeezed their text. *(Fixed 25 September 2026: your grounds are orange on the map and carry an orange dot in the list; each row is one column — name and tag, then city, country and capacity, then the rounds — so nothing breaks mid-word.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 25 September 2026: THE FINAL written beside the final's ground reads oddly. *(Fixed 25 September 2026: a trophy sits over the final's dot instead, and the words are gone.)*)*

*Added 19 September 2026, with the batch 3 playtest:*

~~**P8-100 · "Picked 4 times in 42": tournament matchdays are counted per group.**~~ The Team of the matchday regular award in the World Cup reads "Picked N times in 42", but a tournament run has nowhere near 42 matchdays.

- **The cause (found in the code):** `computeRunStats` (`src/engine/run-stats.ts`) groups the teams of the matchday by each match's label. World Cup group games are labelled `Group A · MD 1`, `Group B · MD 1` and so on, so every group's matchday is its own "round": 12 groups × 3 matchdays = 36, plus the knockout rounds, is about 42. Each of those teams is picked from just the four clubs of one group, so the award counts the wrong thing twice over.
- **The fix:** a round is a matchday of the whole competition. Group-stage matchday 1 is one round across all 12 groups, then each knockout round. The labels shown on matches stay as they are; only the grouping key changes.
- **Check the others too:** the Champions League league phase already groups by matchday. Its knockout legs are keyed per leg (`Round of 16 · Leg 1`); decide whether a two-legged tie is one round or two. The full path's league stage is unaffected.
- **Done when:** the World Cup award reads out of the real number of matchdays (3 group + the knockout rounds played), and `verify-run-stats`/`verify-awards` check the round count per mode.
  *(Done 19 September 2026: a match now carries the `round` it counts toward, separate from its label. World Cup group games share `Group stage · MD n`, so a World Cup has 3 group rounds plus its knockout rounds. Custom-path qualifying legs are named per round ("First Qualifying Round · Leg 1"…); all four used to be "Qualifying" and were merged the same way. Champions League legs stay one round each, as in the real competition. The match lists moved to the DB-free `src/engine/run-matches.ts`; `verify-run-stats` counts every mode's rounds (it reproduces the old 42 when the fix is taken out) and `verify-awards` checks the headline's count.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 19 September 2026.)*

*Added 19 September 2026, with the batch 4 playtest:*

~~**P8-101 · A two-legged tie's second leg is hard to reach from the first.**~~ Opening a tie shows the first leg; getting to the second takes going back and finding it again.

- **What's wanted:** the match sheet's header, in the free space beside the back arrow, gets a leg switcher: an arrow either side and the leg in the middle (`‹ LEG 1 ›`).
- **Where it applies:** every two-legged tie (qualifying, knockout play-off, the Champions League knockouts). A single match shows nothing there.
- **What it switches:** the whole sheet, to the other leg of the same tie, without leaving the page.
  *(Done 19 September 2026: `‹ LEG 1 OF 2 ›` in the match sheet's top bar for every two-legged tie; the arrows switch the whole sheet in place, and the leg label gives way to the score as you scroll. The sheet finds the other leg on its timeline; the run hub, which has none, hands both legs over.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 19 September 2026, with one fix asked for and made the same day: the arrows sat at the bar's edges; they now sit right beside LEG 1 OF 2, and the switcher stops taking taps once the score has replaced it.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 20 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-102 · Ratings calculated the way SofaScore calculates them.**~~ The colours already follow SofaScore (P8-44). The maintainer wants the ratings themselves worked out the way SofaScore works them out.

- **Not in any plan yet:** no document in `docs/` plans it. The ratings today come from `match-detail.ts`'s own weighting.
- **Condition, set by the maintainer:** only if SofaScore's method is freely available. If it's proprietary or only described loosely, this is declined, not approximated.
- **First step:** research what SofaScore publishes about its method and write down, with sources, whether it's enough to implement.
  *(Researched 19 September 2026: **not freely available, so declined under the maintainer's condition.** SofaScore publishes only the frame: a 3–10 scale, every player starting at 6.5, five categories (shooting, passing, dribbling, defending, goalkeeping) judged by their effect on the chance of scoring or conceding, and a Gaussian spread; the formula and weights are proprietary. Sources: sofascore.com/news/sofascore-rating-breakdown-what-goes-into-calculations and corporate.sofascore.com/about/rating. Ours already shares the frame (3–10 clamp, a 6.05 baseline in `match-detail.ts`).)* *(Declined by the maintainer, 19 September 2026, as agreed: the method isn't published)*

~~**P8-103 · A two-legged tie's extra time and penalties can't be seen.**~~ When a second leg goes to extra time and penalties, the app says a shootout happened, but the penalty takers and their kicks aren't shown anywhere. A single-match tie already shows them (the shootout on the match sheet, P8-81). ~~**P8-106 · Difficulty doesn't pay enough.**~~ The hardest settings (11/11) earn only 1.85×, which doesn't feel worth the extra pain. Today the multiplier is linear, `1 + (hardness − 3.9) × 0.12`, clamped to 0.45–1.9×: medium is 1.0×, the easiest is about 0.53×, the hardest 1.85×.

- **Rework the curve** so hardness pays more the higher it goes, and check how the extra difficulty actually changes results, so the reward matches the pain.
- **The multiplier is shared with the server** (`supabase/functions/_shared/score.ts`), so a change moves the leaderboard. Decide what happens to runs already saved (rescored, or kept as they are).
  *(Proposal, 19 September 2026, for the maintainer to decide; no code yet. What the pain is: against an equal side your win rate falls from 33.5% at medium to 9.3% at level 10, and an 89-rated World Cup side's title chance from 4.5% (medium) to 1.5% (hard). It multiplies; the reward only adds. Two curves: **A**, the multiplier doubles every 3.55 points of hardness: easiest 0.47×, easy 0.65×, medium 1.00×, hard 1.83×, 9/11 2.71×, 11/11 4.00×. **B**, doubling every 2.5: 0.34×, 0.54×, 1.00×, 2.36×, 4.11×, 7.16× (today: 0.53×, 0.74×, 1.00×, 1.37×, 1.61×, 1.85×). Recommendation: A. It only changes the score (tiers don't use it). Saved runs: the server scores with `scoreRun`, so rescoring them is a one-off update if wanted.)* *(The maintainer chose curve A, 19 September 2026. Done the same day: `scoreMultiplierFor` in `supabase/functions/_shared/score.ts` now doubles every 3.55 points of hardness, no cap. `verify-difficulty` and `verify-score` pass. The `submit-run` edge function has to be redeployed for the server to score with it. Runs already saved keep their old score unless the maintainer asks for a rescore)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 20 September 2026.)*
