*Added 20 September 2026, with the batch 7 playtest:*

~~**P8-111 · The hazard stripe and misery red collide. One system, not two.**~~ Now that red means a loss, the black-and-white checkered stripe turns up next to it and the two say the same thing twice — most visibly in the simulation screen, between the table and the matchday panel, where your losing result carries a red fill AND a striped edge. Relegation is the other odd one: with green for going up, red is what going down should be, not a stripe.

- **The rule to settle on (proposed, for the maintainer to confirm):**
  - **Misery red** = a bad outcome, or a destructive or failed action: a defeat, elimination, relegation, a red card, a missed penalty, Delete account, abandoning a run, an error.
  - **Volt** = a win, the title, Perfection. **Grey** = a draw.
  - **The stripe** stops meaning "bad" and means **"not available, handle with care"**: a player you can't pick, a hanger out of position, offline, and notices that warn rather than report a result. It also stays as dressing where it isn't next to an outcome (the awards' header band, a title's underline, the Chaos and Cursed labels — those are P8-98's anyway).
- **Every stripe in the app today, and what it becomes:**

| Where                                                                                                         | Today                   | Becomes                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| The loss tag's edge (`Tag variant="loss"`)                                                                  | red fill + striped edge | red fill alone — the collision the maintainer saw |
| Relegation and OUT rows in a table (`ZoneEdge` tone `out`)                                                | stripe                  | red                                                |
| The Misery verdict (`VerdictBlock`, `RunLabel`, the ceremony fill)                                        | stripe                  | red                                                |
| A red card, a penalty not scored (`LiveMatch`)                                                              | stripe                  | red                                                |
| A destructive plate, the abandon screen, a field in error                                                     | stripe                  | red                                                |
| An error notice ("That didn't work", "the pool didn't load")                                                  | striped notice          | red-edged notice                                   |
| A player unavailable, a hanger out of position                                                                | stripe                  | stripe (unchanged)                                 |
| Offline                                                                                                       | stripe                  | stripe (unchanged)                                 |
| A notice that explains rather than reports (Cursed's warning, "no leagues in this build", "nobody here fits") | striped notice          | stripe (unchanged)                                 |
| The awards' header band, a super title's underline, the Chaos/Cursed labels                                   | stripe                  | stripe (dressing)                                  |

- **Done when** every entry above matches, and no screen shows red and the stripe meaning the same thing. *(Done 20 September 2026. Every entry in the table above now matches: the loss tag is red alone, relegation and OUT rows are red, the Misery verdict (the label, the block and the ceremony's pull-across) is red, a red card and a penalty not scored are red, a destructive plate, a field in error and the abandon screen are red, and a notice that reports a failure takes a red edge (`StripedNotice failed`). The stripe keeps only what it now means — not available, handle with care: an unavailable player, a hanger out of position, offline, and notices that warn rather than report (Cursed's, "no leagues in this build", "nobody here fits") — plus its dressing uses (the awards' header band, a title's underline, the Chaos and Cursed labels, which P8-98 rebuilds))*

*Added 20 September 2026, after the maintainer played the Champions League full path end to end (the screenshots are the source for every entry below):*

~~**P8-112 · Flags become real flags, not emoji.**~~ Every flag in the app is a text emoji inside a circle (`RoundFlag`, `flagMap`, `flagForCountry`). National sides keep the flag as their mark — that part is settled — but it should be a proper circular flag IMAGE, the same way clubs now wear scraped crests (P8-12).

- **Why:** an emoji is the platform's font, not ours. It renders differently on every device, it's missing on some Windows browsers (the web build already ships a font polyfill just to draw them), and it sits oddly beside a real crest in the same row.
- **Where:** everywhere `RoundFlag` is used — the World Cup tables, groups and brackets, the draw, the fixtures list, the Europe pages, the club and match pages.
- **The source is already known:** Transfermarkt serves `flagge/head/<countryId>.png` (424 bytes) and `flagge/verysmall/` (175 bytes), and the country ids are a fixed list of about 55 (The Dugout's SCRAPE.md, and it's the cheapest thing on that list). The crest scraper (`scripts/scrape-crests.ts`) gains a flags pass, and `crestFor` gains the national-side case so nothing else changes.
- **Keep the fallback:** when there's no image, the circle keeps the country's code the way it does today. *(Already done as P8-58 (24 September 2026, confirmed): every round flag is a bundled flag image, the emoji only a fallback. What's left is the emoji inside a line of text on the old match sheet (`withFlag`), which the maintainer put in Phase 8.5.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)*

It needs going through **screen by screen**, in the order the run happens, against the screenshots.

Crests at small sizes are the general fault — see P8-119.

- **Done when** every screen of the full path is on the kit, carries the same table, tie row, crest and type as the rest of the app, and none of them opens with a paragraph. *(Done 25 September 2026 (batch 17), the parts that were still open (the bracket and the knockout list were already done by P8-79, crests on the tie rows by P8-156): **Europe's tables** are the kit's own `LeagueTable` now (`CustomUclViewers.tsx`, rewritten): crests on every row, the berths drawn as the table's zones (UCL, PO, Q3, Q2, Q1) with the zone legend, the regular-season/final switch as chips, and the page's subtitle in words (no emoji flag in the text). What each finish earns, on the draw, is on the kit too (it sat on a dark panel of the old design). **The qualifying ladder's** headings are the kit's, one line each ("3 TIES · 1 BYE · WINNERS TO Q3"), not a sentence per round. **Too much text:** the league phase opens with one line in both Champions League modes. **The result page** lost about 100 lines of dead pre-redesign helpers and styles. **Still open:** the full path's missing press, field and pundits steps are P8-120's, which waits for the phase-two plan (P8-71).)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 26 September 2026, from screenshots: the top of every full-path screen was cut off; no crests on the matchday results or on the live tie; the qualifying ladder still read downwards. *(Fixed 26 September 2026: **the cut-off** was `KitScreen`'s status-bar band (added 24 September so a scrolled page can't run under the clock). It's drawn at the screen's own top, and the full path's screens sit under the stage road, so the band covered each page's first lines. `KitScreen` takes `underHeader` now, for a screen with a header above it: no band and no status-bar padding. **Crests on results:** the shared result row's two sides were plain columns, so a crest fell under the name instead of beside it (every result list in the app), and the full path's rows were never given club ids, so every crest was drawn initials. Both fixed. **The scoreline card** (your match, every league and Champions League season) carries both crests now. **The live tie** showed only a nation's flag, so no club tie had any mark: it wears each side's crest or flag now (every knockout, qualifying tie and World Cup match). **The qualifying ladder** has the newest round on top, as the knockouts do (P8-63), live and on the result page.)*)* *(The screenshot fixes above: Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 26 September 2026: your tie's card in the knockouts had no crests, and said KNOCKOUT PLAY-OFF (9TH–24TH) twice. *(Fixed 26 September 2026: the tie card shows each side's crest (or a nation's flag); it had flags only. The round's name is the heading just above it, with its explainer, so the card doesn't repeat it (it stays in what a screen reader says).)*)*

~~**P8-114 · The league-phase draw is missing entirely, and the pots with it.**~~ The Champions League league phase currently appears fully formed: there's no draw, and no pot structure the player can see.

- **What it should be:** the real thing — 36 clubs in four pots of nine, each club drawn two opponents from each pot, two home and two away, with the association rule (no two clubs from the same country, and at most two from any one country in your eight).
- **Copy it from The Dugout**, which already models this; **the official UEFA papers are in The Dugout's docs** (`D:\The Dugout\docs`, the competitions plan and its reference folder) — read them before writing anything, and follow the ids-as-recorded rule from P8-12.
- **Show the pots**: which pot each club is in, and which pot each of your eight opponents came from (the "YOUR EIGHT" list already prints POT 1–4, so the data is there; the draw itself is what's missing).
- **More research first:** this is a research-then-build entry, not a quick one. *(Not in batch 17, on purpose: it's the next batch on its own. What reading The Dugout's version showed (25 September 2026): today's pots are by OVR and each club's eight opponents come from fixed offsets within the pots, so it isn't a draw at all, and there's no association rule. The Dugout's draw needed a real backtracking search (greedy and most-constrained-first both drew 0 of 20 fields, because the format has no slack: 72 pot-1 endpoints against exactly 72) and a feasibility count before searching (`minimumViableCap`: its first version searched an impossible field for 160 seconds and froze the game). Both lessons come over with it.)* *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 18), the draw only. **The rules** (UEFA's 2025–26 draw procedure, from The Dugout's `docs/reference/uefa`): four pots of nine, the title holders top of pot 1; every club draws one opponent at home and one away from each pot; never a club from its own country, at most two from any other. **The engine** is `src/engine/cl-draw.ts`, a backtracking search that always fills the most constrained slot next, with a step budget. Before it searches, **a count** says whether the pots can be drawn at all: a country with K clubs in the field can have at most 9 − K in any pot (every club of that country needs two opponents from each pot, and each other club there can meet only two of them). That's why the real pot 1 had three English clubs, and it's what PoM's 2025 edition broke: potted by rating, it put four English clubs in pot 1 and could never be drawn. So **the pots are nudged** where the count fails: the weakest club of the crowded country swaps with the nearest-rated club of another country in the next pot (the holders never move). Where a field still can't be drawn, the cap rises only as far as the count forces, and the country rule only goes if even that fails; the draw screen says which rule gave. Measured by `scripts/verify-draw.ts` over 600 made-up fields plus the real ones: the real 2025–26 field with its real pots draws under the full rules 60 times in 60, PoM's 2024 and 2025 editions 40 in 40, and 150 in 150 fields shaped like the real one; 8 ms a draw on average and 107 ms at the slowest (the first version averaged 300 ms and took 1.2 s at worst, searching fields the count now turns away at once). It checks two opponents per pot, one home and one away, four and four, no one met twice, the country rule and cap, one match per club per matchday, that the same field draws differently twice, and that the holders stay in pot 1. **The screen** (both Champions League modes, before the league phase): the four pots on screen, your club marked, then your eight drawn one at a time in UEFA's order (pot 1 first, home then away), each lighting up in its pot, with its crest and its country's flag, and the country rule in words; Show all skips; then your eight by matchday as before. The quick-sim tester draws the same way. **Found on the way:** the full path made its whole fixture list again after every league-phase matchday and reset the injury and suspension ledger with it (the setup effect listened to the table, which changes every matchday); it runs once now. **Not here:** pots by real club coefficients (PoM has none; they're by rating, as before) and the matchday schedule's own UEFA rules (no more than two home or away in a row).)* *(The maintainer, 26 September 2026: the draw works, but it looks stale beside The Dugout's. *(Redone 26 September 2026 after The Dugout's `DrawReveal` (its five lessons are in the file's comment): **one card for your club**, named in full, with your eight grouped by the opponent's pot, each pot a block with its own coloured spine (`POT_COLOURS`, the four the old Champions League screens used), and **HOME and AWAY as columns**, your club standing in whichever it plays in; an undrawn opponent **scrambles** (random capitals re-rolling, settling left to right when its ball comes out, so a name's length gives nothing away), drawn in its pot's colour with its crest and flag; **a small bowl** of discs that lifts a ball before each name; **pause and skip**, and a count of how many are out; then **every club's eight**, folded by pot until you open one. One clock drives all the noise, and it stops once the last name has settled. Reduced motion shows the card filled.)*)* *(The redone draw, the pundits' spot-on calls, See the bracket pausing, the landed label and your name on the full path: Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-115 · "Skip to the last matchday" does nothing in the league phase.**~~ In the full path's league phase the skip button is wired to `lpSkipRef`, which is created (`custom-ucl-simulation.tsx` line 247) and **never assigned** — the domestic season's equivalent is assigned (`domSkipRef.current = skipDomesticSeason`), the league phase's isn't. So the confirm appears, you accept, and nothing happens. It needs the league phase's own skip, and that skip must play the matches exactly as watching does (P8-27's `playFixture`). *(Done 25 September 2026 (batch 17): the skip is wired (`lpSkipRef` is assigned to the league phase's skip). And watching and skipping now play every match through one function, the classic league phase's `playFixture` (P8-27): rotation, availability, the lineup-based rating and form all apply. The full path had two copies of its own that left out rotation and form.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 26 September 2026: it skips a little too much, straight on to the knockouts with no time to see the final table. *(Fixed 26 September 2026: the league phase stops on its final table, skipped or watched (watching went on to the knockouts one tick after matchday 8), and **To the knockouts** takes you on when you've read it. A skip now keeps every skipped matchday's results too, so the strip of your results and the results tab have all eight.)*)* *(Stopping on the final table: Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-116 · The tournament's best players are decided by how far their club went.**~~ In the full path a club can play two matches (out in the first qualifying round) or seventeen (into the final), and **every award term is a season total** — goals ×6, assists ×3, man of the match ×4, and the rating term is `(rating − 6.3) × matches played × 2` (`src/engine/stats.ts`). A player who was outstanding over two qualifying legs cannot compete with a steady player who played fifteen more games, so the team of the tournament and the player of the tournament are really a "who went furthest" list. *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

- **What's wanted:** a player who didn't reach the league phase but was genuinely excellent should still be able to make the team, and shouldn't be shut out by volume alone.
- **Ways to weigh it (to decide with the maintainer):** score per match played with a minimum-matches floor; or keep totals but normalise by the matches the player's club actually had available; or split the award (a totals-based "of the tournament" and a rate-based one). The existing carry modifier (a lower finish boosts a player, `CARRY_WEIGHT`) is a different thing and doesn't fix volume.
- **Checked by `verify-awards`:** a two-game star and a fifteen-game steady player should both be able to win, and neither should win every time.
- **The maintainer's direction (22 September 2026):** split it by stage — **qualifying** gets its own recognition, and **the league phase and knockouts** keep theirs. Going further *should* count: UEFA's real team of the season is drawn almost entirely from the clubs that went deepest, so the main awards leaning that way is right. What's wrong is a qualifying-only standout having no award at all to win. So: a qualifying team and player of their own, and the tournament awards measured over the league phase and knockouts. *(Done 25 September 2026 (batch 17), the maintainer's direction: the full path's awards, the team of the tournament and the club awards are measured over the **league phase and knockouts** only, as UEFA's own are; **qualifying has its own player and team** ("Player of qualifying", "The team of qualifying"), measured on the same scoring model over qualifying alone with no finishing carry. The run's stats, player pages, game logs and teams of the matchday still count every match; only the awards are split, in the same pass (two more accumulators), so no match sheet is generated twice. The split is kept with the run (`awards.qualifying`, the best six at each position, and `awards.teams`, the main stage's club lines), so a saved run shows it too. The awards page and the live ceremony show Qualifying under its own heading (first in the ceremony, as it was played). `verify-awards` checks that a two-game qualifying star wins player of qualifying and makes its team but reaches no tournament award, that the club awards read the main stage, and that a run without qualifying has no such section; `verify-run-stats` that exactly the qualifying legs are marked.)*

Today the opponent is always on the same side and only the house/plane icon says which it was. *(Done 25 September 2026 (batch 17): the shared `FixtureRow` takes your club's name and then reads like the scoreline it becomes: your side on the left at home and on the right away, `v` between you until it's played, then the score home–away, with the W/D/L still yours. Used in Your eight in both Champions League modes (before the start and in the live tab). Without your name (the World Cup's neutral grounds) it's the opponent only, as before.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-119 · Crests are missing or broken at small sizes.**~~ Several places still show no crest, and the drawn fallback breaks when it's small.

- **The drawn badge truncates its initials** (`F…`, `U…`, `P…`) at 16–20px: the text is sized from the badge, but the badge is too narrow for three letters plus its padding, so the tail is cut. Either the badge widens, or the initials shorten at small sizes, or the text scales to fit.
- **Clubs with no crest file** fall back to the drawn badge (it's how it's meant to work), but the custom path's field is where most of them are — the scrape resolved 914 of the seeds' club entries, and what's left is largely small-association clubs. Worth a second pass with `REMAP=1` and the missing names listed, so what remains is a known list rather than a surprise.
- **Still missing a crest entirely:** the knockout tie rows on the result pages (old `KnockoutRoundsView`), the bracket, the Europe's-seasons league tables, the champions list ("EUROPE IS DONE" names its winners with no mark), the squad and player pages, and the awards. *(Done 25 September 2026 (batch 17), in part: **the drawn badge** below 24 px shows two letters, not three, with the club-form words dropped first (FC Metz is ME, not FC; Bayern München BM), so nothing is cut to "F…" any more (`shortCrestInitials` in `src/lib/brand.ts`). **Crests added:** every row of Europe's tables and the list of leagues (their champions), with P8-113. **Still open:** a second scrape pass with `REMAP=1` to list the clubs that still have no crest file (it needs the network and a run of the scraper, so it wasn't done here), and crests on the squad and player pages and on the awards' shirts (which show the club's code).)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)* *(What was left of it moves to P8-171.)*

**P8-120 · One version of each step, used by every mode.** The full path is missing steps the other modes have, and where both exist they're different screens doing the same job.

- **The full path has no "the field"** (the strongest-first list of who you're up against) and **no pundits** — the league modes and the tournaments both have them.
- **It also has no press**, where a league run does.
- **The rule:** one version of each step, the better of the two, used everywhere — as the season, the table, the tie row and the match sheet already are. Where the two versions each do something well, combine them rather than picking. *(Not in batch 19: this is P8-71's phase two (one version of each step, shared by every mode), which waits until the rest of Phase 8 is done and the centralisation audit is redone.)*

~~**P8-121 · Sharing a run: the picture AND a link back into the app.**~~ Sharing currently produces the image only. It should offer the image **plus a link**, and that link should open the run: in the app when it's installed (a deep link), or on the web build otherwise.

- **What the link needs:** a run is already saved with an id, so the link is that id; the run page has to load a run that isn't yours to read (the sharing rules from P8-89's run ownership work).
- **Where:** everywhere a run can be shared — the result screens, the run hub, Runs. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 19): runs already had a public link (`/r/<run id>`, with the verdict in its preview, `api/r.ts`), and the result screens put it in the shared text. What was broken: **on a phone the link never went anywhere.** The picture is shared through expo-sharing, which shares a file alone; the text with the link was only the share sheet's title. A phone's sheet can't carry a picture and text together, so they're two plates now: **Share the picture** and **Share the link** (the system sheet, text only). On the web it's one share, words and link, as before. **The link opens the app:** the link's page (`api/r.ts`) hands a phone's visit to the app (`pom://r/<id>`; on Android through Chrome's intent link, which falls back to the web page by itself when the app isn't installed; on iOS the scheme, then the web page if nothing answers), and a computer goes to the web page. The app answers `pom://r/<id>` at `app/r/[id].tsx`: it looks the run up and opens its result screen (the run is public to read, P8-89). **Where:** the result screens and the run hub (`ShareLinkPlate`, one piece). Runs has no share of its own: a run opened from it shares from its result. **To take effect:** the web has to be deployed for the new link page; the app's `pom` scheme is already in every build, so no new build is needed. **Not done:** https links opening the app directly (Android App Links, iOS universal links) need the signing certificate's fingerprint published on the site (`.well-known/assetlinks.json`, from `eas credentials`); until then the page hands over through the scheme.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 26 September 2026: do the credentials part soon. It's P8-174 now.)*

~~**P8-122 · The pundits, checked: show the points too, and make it look like the rest.**~~ The verdict's pundits table reads as a plain list of places and UP/DOWN calls.

- **Add the points** they predicted against the points you actually finished on (P8-13 made the pundits call points, so the number exists), per club and for you.
- **Say more:** what they got right and wrong is one line today ("They got 3 of 18 places exactly right"); it should also name their best call, their worst call, and how they did on you specifically.
- **Visually:** it needs the same pass the rest of the verdict had. In the screenshot its heading also **collides with the screen above it** — the title sits over the previous section's text. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 19): **the points:** each club's row shows where it finished with its crest, what the pundits tipped ("TIPPED 4TH · 61 PTS") and the points it actually got. The pundits' points were already worked out (P8-13) and never shown; a saved run now keeps them beside the places (`highlights.punditPoints`); a run saved before this has no points column. **Say more:** four lines instead of one: how many places they got exactly right, their best call (spot on, then nearest on points), their worst, and how they did on you ("On you: tipped 5th on 55 points. You finished 2nd on 70 points, 3 places better than they said."). **The collision:** the section has room above its heading now. `verify-predictions` checks the four lines, including a saved run without points. (The cups' pundits are P8-165's redo.))* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)* *(The maintainer, 26 September 2026: almost perfect; check the points as well as the places. *(Done 26 September 2026: each call now says **MEGA SPOT ON** when the place and the points were both right, **PLACE SPOT ON** or **POINTS SPOT ON** when one was (gold, with the sparkle, either way), otherwise how far out the place was. A club's points are volt when it beat the pundits' tip, red when it fell short, gold when it hit it exactly. The first summary line counts the points totals and the mega ones too. `callOf` in `src/lib/punditsSummary.ts`, checked by `verify-predictions`.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)* *(Found in the maintainer's screenshot, 27 September 2026: a spot-on call wrapped where it liked, and its sparkle sat on the points figure beside it. *(Fixed 27 September 2026: a spot-on call reads on two short lines, PLACE / SPOT ON, right-aligned inside its column.)*)*

~~**P8-123 · One type system, everywhere.**~~ Several screens still use the platform font rather than the kit's (the older dark screens, the match sheet's tables, the knockout views, the Europe pages). Every piece of text in the app should come from the kit's type scale and its four families (the super, the tag mono, the body and its weights) — no screen should be drawing text in the system font or setting its own sizes. This is the type half of P8-104's sweep and should be done with it. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 19): 220 pieces of text in 14 files still drew with React Native's own Text: some in the system font (no family at all), most at sizes of their own (9, 10, 12, 20, 56): the match sheet and its tables (135 of them), the old result pieces, the lineup pitch, the shootout, the squad summary and a few old components. The Europe pages and knockout views were already on the kit (P8-113, P8-79). They all draw through one kit piece now, `ScaleText`: a size snaps to the nearest step of the kit's scale (never under the tag's 11), a missing family becomes the body face by the weight it asked for, `fontWeight` goes (each weight is its own family, and on Android a custom font with a weight set can fall back to the system font), and a line height keeps its proportion. So no text in the app is in the system font or at a size off the scale. **It's a floor, not a redesign:** the 9 and 10 px text in the match sheet's dense tables is 11 now, so those tables are a little bigger and may need a look; when a screen is rebuilt it moves to KitText and its tokens.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)*

*Added 22 September 2026:*

~~**P8-124 · Shirt numbers mean the position.**~~ "Your shape" numbers its shirts by slot order (`slot.slotIndex + 1`, `formation-select.tsx`), so a CAM can be a 7 and a striker a 3. Football reads numbers as positions: the keeper is 1, the full-backs 2 and 3, the centre-backs 4 and 5 (6 in a back three), the holding midfielder 6, the wide right 7, the central midfielder 8, **the striker 9, the number ten 10**, the wide left 11. Give each position its traditional number, with a stable rule for shapes that have two of one kind (two strikers: 9 and 10; a second holding midfielder: 4 or 8). Wherever a shirt shows a number — the shape, the draft pitch, the awards' pitch — use the same map. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 19): a shared map (`shirtNumbers`, `src/engine/formations.ts`): each position takes the first free number from its own list, in a fixed order so the iconic numbers go first: keeper 1, right-back 2, left-back 3, wide right 7, wide left 11, the number ten 10, strikers 9 (then 10, then 11), centre-backs 5, 4 (6 in a back three), holding midfield 6 (a second one 8), central midfield 8, 6, 10. So a 4-3-3 is 1, 2, 5, 4, 3, 8, 6, 10, 7, 9, 11 and a 4-4-2's strikers are 9 and 10. The shape screen is the only place a shirt shows a number today. `verify-shapes` checks every formation: all different, the keeper 1, a striker 9, a lone ten 10, the full-backs 2 and 3.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-125 · A player's nation and club behind him.**~~ Both on the phone and on PC:

- **In the formation:** each filled square carries the player's **national flag as its background**, at low opacity. A club player gets his **club's crest** behind him instead, with a small national flag in a corner.
- **When a player is held or picked:** the **whole screen** takes on his mark — the flag for a national side's player, the crest for a club player — as a quiet background, the way the spin already tints the club card (P8-05).
- **The hold line says who it is:** "Holding Bellingham" becomes the player's **full name** ("Holding Jude Bellingham"), since a surname alone can be two different people in one squad.
- **Depends on** P8-112 (flag images) and P8-12 (crests); an emoji flag can't be a background. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 19): **in the formation:** each filled spot has the player's mark faintly behind him (`MarkBackdrop`, one piece): a national side's player (the World Cup) his flag; a club player his club's crest, with his nation's flag small in the corner. **Held or picked:** the whole draft screen takes his mark as a quiet background while you hold him or place him. **Full names:** "Holding Jude Bellingham", and the placement line the same. A drafted player keeps his club's id now for the crest; runs drafted before it have none, so their spots show the flag only.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-126 · More achievements, and more creative ones.**~~ Today's achievements are about beating each mode on each difficulty. Add ones that aren't tied to a mode, that reward a way of playing:

- win a league with an XI **all from one nation**;
- win with an XI **all under 21**;
- the maintainer's two, and ones in the same spirit to decide together — for example an XI of eleven different nations, a title with the weakest squad in the league, a perfect season with ratings hidden, a trophy with nobody over 28, a whole XI from one club's history.
- Each is checked from the saved run (the squad, the result and the difficulty are all stored), so it can be computed from past runs too. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 19): **Feats**, a new section on the achievements screen, checked from every saved run (past ones too), with how many times each was earned: **One nation** (win with an eleven all from one country) and **The kids** (win with an eleven all 21 or under, ages as of the season each was drafted from) are the maintainer's; **Eleven nations**, **No veterans** (nobody over 28), **One club** (a whole eleven from one club's history), **Blind and perfect** (Perfection, or the cup, with the ratings hidden) and **Invincibles** (a league won without a defeat) are proposals in the same spirit, to keep, change or drop. Rules in `src/lib/feats.ts` (pure), checked by `scripts/verify-feats.ts`. A run without a full eleven or without ages can't earn a squad feat. **Left out:** "the title with the weakest squad in the league" needs the league's other squads, which a saved run doesn't keep. Feats aren't among the trophies on a profile yet.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-127 · Back from a match sheet can loop the abandon screen (found on PC).**~~ During a simulation, with play stopped, opening a match and going back can drop you into the abandon-the-run screen over and over. Seen on the web build; not yet checked on a phone.

- **Likely cause (to confirm with logging before changing anything):** on web, `useSimBackGuard` pushes a sentinel history entry and listens for `popstate` every time the simulation screen **regains focus**. Leaving the match sheet is itself a history step back, and its `popstate` can arrive **after** the simulation screen has refocused and re-armed — so the guard reads the return from the match sheet as the player pressing back, opens the abandon screen, and each return re-arms it again.
- **Check** Android's hardware back through the same path too, and add the case (open a match, return, return again) to whatever P8-26's back handling is tested with. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 19), **to confirm on PC**: the cause is as the entry guessed, as far as reading the code goes. Every return to the simulation pushed another history sentinel, and a `popstate` from leaving the match sheet that landed just after the screen re-armed read as a back press. The fix: a `popstate` within 400 ms of the guard arming is treated as that return (re-armed quietly, no Abandon); the sentinel is pushed only when the current entry isn't one already, so they no longer pile up; and it keeps the navigator's own history state, so stepping back off it reads to the navigator as the same screen. A dev-only log line (`[back-guard] popstate … ms after arming`) says which case each one was, so the next time it happens the console shows whether this was the cause. Android's hardware back goes through BackHandler, which is only listening while the simulation is in front, so the match sheet's back never reaches it.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 26 September 2026.)*

So a few runs wear a green or red strip and most don't, which reads as an inconsistency rather than a verdict. Either every run's label says its outcome the same way, or the strips go. **Decide it inside the Runs and Ranks redesign** (P8-85, P8-99 and the run pages in P8-95), since how a run is shown in a list is being redone there anyway. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 20), decided: every run's label says its outcome, the same way. One edge on every label: volt for Perfection, red for Misery, and the draw's grey for everything between (the verdict is in the spoken label too). The strips aren't gone; they're on every run now, so they read as a scale rather than a glitch.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-129 · The man of the match award.**~~ The maintainer asked for it. The engine already has an award called **"Man of the match, most often"** (`key: 'motm'` in `awards.ts`: most man-of-the-match wins, levels broken by average rating). **To settle with the maintainer:** whether it's that award that should be shown more prominently (on Awards Night and the verdict), or a **man of the match moment after every one of your matches**, named at full time the way the match sheet names it. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 20), the second of the two: **a man of the match after every one of your matches**, named under your scoreline card at full time (the league modes, the classic league phase, the full path's domestic season and league phase), with his rating square: the same player the match sheet crowns, from the same seed (`ManOfTheMatch`, beside the sheet's own hook). The season's "Man of the match, most often" stays on Awards Night. **Found on the way:** the full path's results didn't pass who each side had rested to the match sheet, so its sheet could regenerate a different eleven from the one that played; they carry it now. Not on the knockout ties' card: a two-legged tie has two men of the match, one per leg, on each leg's sheet.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-130 · Later, bigger matches count for more.**~~ For the positional awards (forward, midfielder, defender of the season) and the team of the season: a performance in a final or a title run-in should weigh more than the same performance on matchday 1, the way real voters remember the big nights. *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026: "Everything else works as well" after batch 20, which built it.)*

- **Measured, not invented:** no imaginary voters. Each match gets an **importance weight** from its stage (a knockout leg over a league-phase game, a final over everything, the last matchdays of a close league over the first), the award score multiplies each match's contribution by it, and the breakdown shows the weighting so the result can be checked.
- It pairs with P8-116: the stage split decides who's eligible for which award, the weighting decides how much each match counts inside it.
- **Checked by `verify-awards`:** a player whose best games came in the knockouts should edge an otherwise identical player whose best games were early. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 20): each match has a weight from its stage (a final 1.5, a semi-final 1.3, a quarter-final 1.2, the round of 16, the round of 32 and the knockout play-off 1.1, a league's last five matchdays 1.1, everything else 1), and a player's award score (and the positional awards' scores) is scaled by how much of his own contribution came in the weighted games: his rating above an ordinary 5.5 in each, so a quiet final lifts nobody and a great one does. The breakdown shows it as its own line ("Big matches ×1.08"). It weighs only the games the award is for (the full path's qualifying has its own, P8-116). `src/engine/importance.ts`; `verify-awards` checks that the big-night player edges an identical early one, that the weighting shows and reaches the positional scores; `verify-defender` still passes.)*

They want it to feel more like football.

- **Measure it:** before calling Phase 8's identity done, put the build in front of the same people again and ask the same question. *(Not built as such: it's this phase's premise heard from outside, and each item that answers it is its own (P8-125's marks behind the players, the draw's redo, crests everywhere, P8-132's crest of your own). It stays open until the same people have seen the build again, as the entry says.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-132 · Your own crest.**~~ Build one, or upload an image, in a proper edit mode that saves it: shape, colours, a shirt device, initials or a mark, the way the drawn badge is built (P8-12) but chosen rather than generated.

- **A setting** makes it replace your side's mark everywhere, the Champions League and the World Cup included.
- **Stored with the account**, so it follows you between devices, and on each run so an old run still shows the crest it was played with. *(Done 26 September 2026 (batch 20): **the editor** (Profile editor → Your crest, `app/crest-edit.tsx`): a shape (square, shield, round), two colours from the palette, a shirt device (plain, hoop, halves, sash, chevron, band) and up to three letters, with a preview at full size and at the size it has in a table row; or **a picture** instead (the avatar's picker, uploaded to your own folder). **Where it shows:** on the side you field in the league modes; with **Everywhere** on, on your side in the Champions League and the World Cup too (over a nation's flag as well). Every place the game draws a club's mark (the kit's `Crest` and `TeamMark`) swaps it in on its own. **Kept:** with the account, on the look (`profile_looks`), and **with each run** (the run's highlights, added where every save passes), so an old run still shows the crest it was played with, yours or another player's; another player's is read defensively. **To apply:** run `supabase/crest.sql` once (three columns); until then the editor says so on saving. `scripts/verify-crest.ts` checks the letters, a strange stored row made safe, which modes take the crest, and a saved run's own crest.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)* *(Fixed 27 September 2026: changing the shape didn't redraw the crest (a clip path whose outline changes in place doesn't redraw everywhere); the drawing is keyed by its shape now. The rest of the maintainer's notes on it are P8-175.)*
