~~**P8-137 · The simulation's pace is the same on every device.**~~ World Cup group games play noticeably faster on PC than on a phone: the matchday loop runs as fast as the device renders. Cap it to a fixed pace per speed setting, so slow, normal and fast mean the same thing everywhere — slower on PC on purpose, never faster than the phone. (The delay when switching tabs during a simulation is Phase 9's performance work.) *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 21): the live clock keeps wall time. Each minute used to wait its 26 ms AFTER the render the last minute caused, so a phone's slower render added to every minute and a PC played the same match about twice as fast. Now each tick works out when it was due and, when it's late, moves on by the minutes that have passed, so every device keeps the same pace. The pace is set near the phone's real one, as asked (slower on a PC, never faster than the phone): 42 ms a minute, about 3.8 s a match plus the beats on goals, one constant (`MS_PER_MIN`, `LiveMatch.tsx`). A dev-only log line at full time gives the real duration, to tune it against. A pause never fast-forwards: the clock's wall time restarts after one.)* *(The maintainer, 27 September 2026: on a PC the World Cup was "still fast as hell"; and pausing a World Cup game said it paused while the clock ran on. *(Fixed 27 September 2026 (batch 22), three causes. The World Cup's live match never heard the page's Pause (it had no `hold` from it) and never heard the speed setting either; it takes both now (`hold={!isPlaying}`, and the speed's own pace). The wall clock's restart after a pause or a new period ran after the tick that should have used it, so resuming jumped the clock forward; it runs first now. And 42 ms a minute was still too quick: the pace is 60 ms a minute on normal (about 5.4 s a match plus the beats on goals), 90 on slow and 35 on fast (`LIVE_MS_PER_MIN`, `LiveMatch.tsx`), the same on every device. Checked for the same pattern elsewhere: the knockout live matches (the classic Champions League and the full path) have no Pause of their own; they hold while scrolled away, as before.)*)*
*Added 22 September 2026, with the batch 9 playtest:* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-138 · The press, reworked again.**~~ The press has to read the season the way a supporter does, from the form as much as the table. Show a team's form with its results. New kinds of story, each measured, not invented:

- **Runs:** unbeaten runs, winning and losing runs, and the break of one.
- **Form that won't hold:** a side that can't keep its form for more than five matches; a zig-zag side (win, loss, win, loss).
- **Character:** the draw specialists; the high- and the low-scoring sides; the sides that concede a lot, and the ones that concede almost nothing.
- **Players:** a player's form over his last matches, not only his season; any match rated 9.8 or higher is a story of its own.
  Short version: another full rework of the press statements, on top of P8-18's kinds. *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 21), from the results as much as the table, each measured: **runs** (a streak now says how long it is; an unbeaten run of eight inside a season that has had a defeat; the end of a run, only when it was news: six straight wins or ten unbeaten, measured, since ending any five-win run was the commonest story of all), **form that won't hold** (six results alternating win and defeat; two blocks of five nine points or more apart), **character** once a third of the season is played (goal-fests at 3.6 goals a game or more, tight games at 1.7 or fewer, leaky at 2.2 conceded a game, a fortress at 0.6 or fewer; said once in most of a season), and **the players**: a player's form (8.0 or better over his last five appearances, all in the last seven matchdays) and **any match rated 9.8 or more, a story of its own** (like an injury: from the first matchday, outside the one-a-round cap). **A team's form with its results:** every form story freezes the club's last five (result, score, opponent, home or away), and the story page shows them. The round's ratings come from the same regenerated match sheets as the team of the matchday (`roundLines`, computed once for both), kept beside the run's history rather than in it, so a saved run doesn't grow. `verify-press` checks each new kind against what happened, over 400 seasons.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026, with one note: a story about one match (a 10 out of 10, and anything else that's really about a game) should show the match, not the table. *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 22): a masterclass, a thrashing, a giant-killing and the end of a run each freeze their match with the story (both sides, the score, the matchday) and show it in place of the table: on the story page as a match card with the crests (and, for a masterclass, the man of the match with his rating), in the season's press list as one match line. On a saved run the card opens the match sheet; mid-season it's the card alone. `verify-press` checks that each of those stories carries its match and that the score is the one played.)*)*

~~**P8-139 · FotMob is the reference for every stat.**~~ For everything the sheet keeps and everything worth telling, FotMob is the source to copy: which stats exist, what they're called, how they're grouped, and what's interesting enough to surface (the first place this already applied is P8-33's feed and timeline). *(A standing reference rather than a build: the press's new form strip follows FotMob's form guide (the result, the score, the opponent, home or away).)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-140 · Press stories wouldn't open during the simulation.**~~ *(Fixed 22 September 2026, to confirm: a story tapped while the season was still playing did nothing. The press row wasn't tappable at all mid-season, and the story page only read the stories kept on a finished run. The rows open the story now, and the season hands its live press to the story page (`src/lib/livePress.ts`, the same module-scope hand-off the match sheet uses) as each matchday lands.)* *(The maintainer, 23 September 2026: opening a press story during the simulation crashed — "The result of getSnapshot should be cached", then "Maximum update depth exceeded". *(Fixed 23 September 2026: the story page read the press with a store selector that ended `?? []`, which returns a new empty array on every read; zustand took that as a change on every render and looped. Mid-season there's no stored press yet, so it was exactly the path this fix opened. The empty case now lives outside the selector, as one shared constant. No other selector in the app builds a fresh value.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-141 · "Awards Night" sometimes sat off to the left.**~~ Sometimes the awards page showed only "Awards" and "off" pushed left, sometimes the title centred as it should. *(Fixed 22 September 2026, to confirm: the loading and failed states used the largest display size, which wrapped on a narrow phone into a left-aligned block ("AWARDS / NIGHT" breaking badly); they now use the next size down and are centred.)* *(The maintainer, 23 September 2026: it still happens, at random, so it wasn't the loading title. *(Fixed 23 September 2026, second go: the real cause is the night's own header. The title shared a row with the "3 / 14" counter, and how wide that counter is depends on the run (how many awards it has, which one is showing); when it was wide, "AWARDS NIGHT" didn't fit, wrapped, and only "AWARDS" showed, off to the left — hence "random". The title now has its own centred line, is always a single line (it shrinks to fit rather than wrap, on the loading screen too), and the counter sits beside the ticks it counts.)*)* *(The maintainer, 24 September 2026, with a screenshot of the screen meant all along: mid-night, a winner's name printed over the phone's clock. *(Fixed 24 September 2026: the status bar is see-through on Android, and the page's content only STARTED below it — scrolled, it ran up under the clock. Every scrolling screen (`KitScreen`) now has a band of its ground's colour behind the status bar, so nothing reads under it anywhere in the app. The same screenshot showed the manager award's runners-up losing their names — a long headline squeezed the club out of its row — so club runners-up now read name over headline, like the player runners-up.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 24 September 2026.)*
*Added 22 September 2026, with the batch 10 notes:*

~~**P8-142 · Your club's colours, chosen on your profile.**~~ Your side wears the colours of the club it replaced (a league run as SD Huesca's navy and red), on the momentum graph, the stat bars and the feed. It should wear yours. The profile needs two colour pickers, a main and a secondary colour, saved with the profile, and every place a club's colours are used takes yours for your side (the team colours of P8-49, the colourway tape, the crest of P8-132). Pick from a curated set that reads on both grounds, rather than a free wheel that can pick an unreadable navy. *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 21): **Your club's colours** in the profile editor: a main and a second colour from the same curated palette as the crest (the palette's own colours, by id). Your side wears them wherever a club's colours are drawn, in place of the club it took over: the match sheet's bars, the momentum graph and the feed (the team colours of P8-49, which still lift a dark colour until it reads). With or without a crest of your own, in the league modes, and everywhere with the crest's Everywhere setting. A new crest design starts from them. Kept with the crest on the look row, and with each run. One control for the swatches now, in the kit (`Swatches`), shared with the crest editor. **To apply:** run `supabase/side-colours.sql` (one column); until then the profile still saves and says the colours are waiting. The colourway tape is the mode's, not a club's, so it doesn't change.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-143 · The line under your club in the season table.**~~ *(Fixed 22 September 2026, to confirm: crossing a zone line drew an orange tape under your row for 700 ms. When the next matchday landed inside that window, the reset was cancelled and the tape stayed for good — the "weird line" under your club. The tape is gone; crossing a zone still gives the haptic, and the movement column says the rest.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-144 · Ratings, rethought.**~~ They aren't broken, but they should be more realistic, and P8-102's research should finally be used. A hat-trick can't be a 9.5; it's a 10. **Show a 10 as "10", never "10.0"**, and use whole numbers wherever the number is whole — in the rating legends too ("3", not "3.0"). Base the model on how SofaScore describes and weights its ratings (a real reference link, so the research is written down), start from the same place SofaScore starts every player, and decide what each action is worth from there. First step: read P8-102 and SofaScore's own explanation, write down what our model does differently, then propose the changes. *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 21), the two firm asks and the research. **A hat-trick is a 10** (three or more goals, unless he also scored an own goal or was sent off); `verify-match-detail` checks every one (157 in its run). **Whole numbers shown whole:** `formatRating` already did ("10", never "10.0"); the places that printed ratings their own way now go through it: the player page's rating graph and its scale (the "3.0" and "10.0" legend), its latest figure, and the match sheet's rating column. **What our model does differently from SofaScore's published frame** (P8-102's sources; the formula itself isn't published): SofaScore starts every player at **6.5**, ours at **6.05**; our overall average rating is **6.28** (measured, `verify-match-detail`); SofaScore judges actions by their effect on the chance of scoring or conceding and describes a Gaussian spread, ours adds fixed weights per action and a small uniform wobble; both clamp to 3–10. **Proposed, for the maintainer to decide:** start at 6.5 and re-weigh so a quiet, ordinary game reads about 6.5 rather than 6.1; value shots and chances by their expected goals rather than a flat bonus; make the wobble Gaussian. It would move every rating colour, which is why it's proposed rather than switched on.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*
*Added 23 September 2026, with the batch 10 playtest:*

~~**P8-145 · Things that live and move on their own.**~~ The maintainer on the pundits' sparkle (P8-38): it "makes it feel more alive, great call, make more like these". A principle for the rest of the phase: wherever it fits, give a screen something that lives and moves without the player doing anything — a quiet, staggered, occasional motion (a twinkle on an exact call, a breath on a live element), never a loop that demands attention, always still under reduced motion. Candidates to look at: the champion's row on a final table, a record on the player page, the verdict's tier, a hot streak in the press, your best player on the team of the matchday. It also answers some of P8-131's "bland and soulless". *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 21): the pundits' twinkle is the kit's now (`Twinkle`): quiet, occasional, staggered, still under reduced motion, gold for the best or the exactly right. It lives on: **the champion's row** of the league's final table, **a Perfection verdict** beside its word, **the round's best player** on the team of the matchday, and **the hot press stories** (a streak, an unbeaten run, a player in form, a masterclass, the champions). Not yet: a record on the player page (no records are kept yet: P8-150).)* *(The maintainer, 27 September 2026: make the twinkle a proper icon, still yellow, not an emoji; and the animation drains a lot of resources. *(Fixed 27 September 2026 (batch 22): the star is drawn (a four-pointed star in the kit's gold with an ink edge, the same on every platform), not the ✦ character. The cost: each star ran an endless animation whose resting part was a 1.9 s "animate from 1 to 1", so every star on a screen updated every frame, forever. Now each star does one short burst (half a second), then nothing at all until a timer starts the next one three to five seconds later, staggered so the stars on one screen don't pulse together. Still under reduced motion.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-146 · One rating square, everywhere.**~~ A player's rating should look the same anywhere in the app: the square in its rating colour, as on the match sheet — on the team-of-the-matchday pitch too. The team of the season and every other rating-based pick shows the ratings; every player page has them; the Player of the Season card shows his (and the runners-up's), so who was better than whom reads from the colour. *(Done 23 September 2026: `RatingSquare` in the kit (`md` and `sm`, one or two decimals) replaced ten separate copies — the match sheet's player rows, top-rated and lineup pitch, the Deep Match, the club and run pages, the player page's matches and his season average, the squad summary. The formation pitch draws it for every player with a rating: the match rating on a team of the matchday, the season average on the team of the season (beside the score that picked him). Award cards show it on the winner and every runner-up. Every rating goes through `formatRating`, so a whole number is written whole — "10", never "10.0" — which is P8-144's display rule, applied now; the rating model itself is still P8-144's.)* *(The maintainer, 23 September 2026: to be looked at again during the centralisation item (P8-50), with the formation.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*
*Added 23 September 2026:*

~~**P8-147 · LAST TIME and Again were two systems.**~~ The LAST TIME tag on the mode and difficulty screens read the game store's current mode and difficulty (overwritten by the next pick, wiped by a reload); Home's Again read the newest saved run from the database (which a guest never has). *(Done 23 September 2026: one "last run" — mode and difficulty — kept in the persisted settings store and written when a finished run reaches its result screen (`useRunSave`, guests included). The two tags and Again all read it. The difficulty tag shows only when the last run was the same mode.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-148 · Settings aren't kept over a reload.**~~ The maintainer, 23 September 2026: switching a setting and reloading brings the default back. The store code is right (written on every change, read at start-up), and the login survives a reload through the same AsyncStorage — for a real account. First check: the console on start-up for "[settings] load failed" or "[settings] save failed"; if AsyncStorage's native module is missing from the current dev build, both fail and a guest session is quietly recreated each launch as well. `src/lib/mmkv.ts` is still the "temporary stub — replace with mmkv after dev build", and react-native-mmkv is installed: moving settings (and later the run) onto MMKV is the planned fix either way. P8-147's last run lives in the same store, so it shares this. *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 22): settings are on MMKV now. It reads synchronously, so the saved settings are in place before the first screen draws; the old path read AsyncStorage in a promise, and anything that read or wrote before it landed saw the defaults and could save them over yours. Settings saved the old way move across once, on the first launch. If a build lacks the native module, settings fall back to AsyncStorage and the console says so. The login stays on AsyncStorage (moving it would sign everyone out once). `src/lib/mmkv.ts`, `src/store/settingsStore.ts`.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-149 · What a reload loses.**~~ "Reload" is the Expo dev server's **r** (or shaking the phone → Reload): it restarts the JavaScript without restarting the app. For a player the equivalent is the app being killed in the background, or closing and reopening it. What survives today: the login (Supabase's session, AsyncStorage) and, when P8-148 is fixed, the settings. **What's lost: everything in the game store** — a run in progress (the draft, the placement, a season half-played, a result not yet saved), the pundits' picks, the live press, the match sheet's hand-offs. A finished run is saved to the database as soon as its result screen has its numbers (for an account), so only unfinished runs are lost. The fix is a persisted run: save the game store's run on each step (draft pick, matchday), restore it on launch, and offer "Continue your run" on Home. It overlaps with P8-96 (saved runs missing things). *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 22), the part that can honestly be picked up again: the run up to its kick-off — mode, difficulty, shape, every pick and the bench, the rerolls used — is written to the device on every change (`src/lib/runKeeper.ts`, on MMKV). Home then offers **Continue your run**, straight back to the draft with the picks in their slots, or **Let it go**. Not a season half-played: its matchdays live in the season screen, and restarting a season from matchday 1 would re-roll every result, the exploit the back guard exists to stop. A run that was already drawn into its competition is said once, plainly ("your last run was stopped during its season, so it's gone"), and let go. A tester's quick-sim run is never kept.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026, with one note: it looks bad. *(Fixed 27 September 2026: the kept run was put inside Home's row of plates, so on a phone it squeezed Start a run off the screen and cut Again in half. It sits above the row now: its line (YOUR ALL TIME RUN · 1 PICKED) with Let it go beside it, then Continue your run as the one orange plate, and Start a run steps down to a secondary plate until the kept run is continued or let go.)*)*

**P8-150 · The career screen, reworked.** One of the last things this phase does: rework the career screen and keep more stats over every run — records, bests, per-mode histories, the players you draft most, how you do against the pundits over time. *(Batch 22, 27 September 2026: left for last, as planned; it pairs with P8-167.)* *(Done 28 September 2026 (batch 23), with P8-167: the career is worked out from every run you've saved (`summarise` in `src/lib/careerSummary.ts`, checked by `verify-career`): runs, trophies, perfect seasons, runners-up, matches and goals; your best run and your best tier; records (the best score, the best league finish, the most wins in a league run, the fewest defeats in a full season, the most goals in a run, the hardest run won, and the run that proved the pundits most wrong); every mode with its runs, trophies and best as bars; **against the pundits**, where they had you and where you finished, run by run, as a chart (every save now keeps the panel's place for you, `punditsOnYou`); the players you've drafted most; and your players' boards. **Fixed on the way** (28 September 2026): a league run's save wrote its highlights and then replaced them with the pundits' place alone, so a league run saved since P8-150 lost its biggest win, press, pundits' table and medical table when opened again; and the World Cup's save took the pundits' place without writing it. Both are right now; runs saved in between keep what they lost.)*
*Added 23 September 2026, with batch 11:*

~~**P8-151 · One tap, one screen.**~~ The maintainer, 23 September 2026: you could double-tap something and open it twice. *(Done 23 September 2026: fixed once for the whole app rather than at 83 call sites. `src/lib/navGuard.ts` wraps `router.push`, `navigate` and `replace`: the same navigation fired again within 0.8 s is dropped, and every navigation marks the app busy; `NavGuard` in the root layout lays a clear layer over everything that takes every tap until the new screen has arrived (released the moment the navigation state moves, or after 2 s whatever happens), and shows the kit's loader if that takes a beat. A screen's own redirect as it mounts (a missing run sending you home) still goes through — only a person's second tap is blocked.)* *(The maintainer, 24 September 2026: there should be a loading bar the moment you tap — a darker press was the only feedback. *(Done 24 September 2026: the bar shows at once, full width across the top of the screen, the kit's own loader (the same sliding block as a plate's), until the new screen arrives.)*)* *(The maintainer, 24 September 2026: the bar should show for buttons that don't go to another screen too (Home, and the rest). *(Done 24 September 2026: every plate, list row and the back control sends a pulse — the same bar across the top for 0.45 s, blocking nothing — so any press is acknowledged. Going back (`router.back`, `dismiss`, `dismissAll`) now counts as a navigation too: the bar and the tap-blocking layer until the screen arrives.)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-152 · Seasons, with ranks and badges.**~~ Runs and the leaderboards split into seasons that follow the real football calendar, three months each: for example **Summer** 1 June – 31 August, **Autumn** 1 September – 30 November, and so on through the year. Pick the start date when the feature is built (call it Season 0 for now; with the likely public release, Summer 2027 would really be Season 1). Every season is defined ahead of time with its dates and a name of its own — about a hundred hand-written names to cover the next 25 years ("A summer of perfection"). The runs and ranks screens get a season picker: the live season, and every one before it.

- **Badges:** one for taking part, and prestige tiers by final rank — **Top 1000, 500, 250, 100, 50, 30, 15, 10, 5, 3, 2 and 1** — each with its own look, so a badge says at a glance what it was.
- **How a badge reads** (the maintainer's sketch):
  > A summer of perfection
  > Season 1
  > 1.6.2027 – 31.8.2027
  > TOP 50
  > #42
  >
- Needs the database (P8-153): a seasons table (id, number, name, start, end), the season stamped on every run, rank snapshots at each season's end, and the badges each player earned.
  *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 22), without a migration. A season is a date range, defined in the app (`src/data/seasons.ts`): **Season 1 is Summer 2027** (1.6.2027 – 31.8.2027, "A summer of perfection"), then a hundred seasons of three months to Spring 2052, each with its own name, twenty-five for each quarter. **Season 0, "Before the whistle"**, is the pre-release season: every run until 31 May 2027. Seasons change at midnight Slovak time, like the ranking week. **Ranks** has a Season board beside All time and This week: pick the live season or any before it, with its name, dates and FINAL or LIVE; it's the same board read between the season's dates (`until` joined `since` on the board's filter). **Runs** has a season filter. **Badges** are on the profile, one per season played: the name, the season, the dates, the tier and the place, as the sketch has it. The tiers look different by band (label grey for Top 1000–250, black for 100–30, pitch green for 15–5, volt for 3–1) and by one to three bars inside a band, with the twinkle on first place; taking part is plain. The live season's badge shows too, dashed and marked SO FAR, so it can be seen now, before any season has finished. A place is the best run's place among runs, as on the rest of the board. A finished season's board can't change (a run is stamped when it's saved), so a badge worked out on open is the one it will always be; the ceiling is two small queries per season played, and a seasons table with rank snapshots (P8-153) replaces that when it matters. `verify-seasons` checks the calendar (contiguous, three months, midnight in Slovakia, the sketch's dates, a leap February) and the place-to-tier mapping.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*

**P8-153 · Some of this phase needs the database.** Noting it now so it's planned, not discovered: several P8 items can't be done in the app alone. *(Batch 22, 27 September 2026: a planning note. P8-152 was built without a migration (see there); the others still need theirs.)* *(28 September 2026, the state at the end of Phase 8. **Written and waiting to be run** in Supabase's SQL editor: `supabase/side-colours.sql` (P8-142/P8-177), `supabase/pin.sql` (P8-168), `supabase/profile-plus.sql` (P8-175/P8-178) and `supabase/clubs.sql` (P8-181); each part of the app says plainly when its table isn't there yet. **The edge function** `submit-run` needs redeploying for the Europa League, Conference League and full-path score ladders (P8-172, P8-52). **The bundled database** is at version 15 (the Europa and Conference League fields), so a new build copies it. Still open from the list below: P8-37's managers, the national teams' colours (the flags' own colours stand in, P8-163), and P8-55's run lists.)*

- **P8-152 seasons:** a seasons table, the season on each run, end-of-season rank snapshots, earned badges.
- **P8-37 real managers:** a manager per club-season, scraped (a column or table in the bundled player DB).
- **P8-142 your colours** and **P8-132 your crest:** saved with the profile (Supabase).
- **P8-149 a run that survives a reload** and **P8-96 saved runs missing things:** more of the run in the saved row, or a run-in-progress record.
- **P8-55's run lists:** the league id on the saved run, so the list can wear the league's colours.
- **National teams' colours** (P8-49 for the World Cup): colours for the nations in the bundled DB.
  Supabase changes go through migrations; the bundled SQLite gets a new `DB_VERSION` (see the DB versioning notes).
  *Added 24 September 2026:*

**P8-154 · The website.** The maintainer, 24 September 2026: "This website is something you cannot undershoot." Exceptional, not a template. The one idea taken from 38-0.app is its social proof, done differently: a **live counter** of seasons played, counting real rows in Supabase and flipping its digits as runs finish anywhere. The full brief — what the site is for, what to take from 38-0 and what not to, the counter's safe design (a trigger-kept `public_counters` row with Realtime on that table only), the design bar, the skills to run it through in order, a Claude Design prompt to see a direction, and the build prompt — is in [`docs/website/WEBSITE-BRIEF.md`](../website/WEBSITE-BRIEF.md). Needs a migration (P8-153). *(Batch 22, 27 September 2026: left for its own session, from its brief.)* *(27 September 2026: it's built as **Phase 10 · The landing page**, with this brief as that phase's creative brief; how it measures itself is in [`docs/Web Analytics & Speed Insights.md`](<../Web%20Analytics%20%26%20Speed%20Insights.md>) §5.)*

~~**P8-155 · Player of the Season by position.**~~ The award engine again: Player of the Season heavily favours attackers — a bias real football has too, but the game shouldn't copy it blindly. Proposal: each position is judged on its own stats (the positional scores P8-36 built — defender, full-back, defensive midfielder — plus ones for keepers, attacking midfielders, wingers and strikers), each normalised against its own position's field, so the Player of the Season is whoever was most exceptional for what they are, not whoever scored most. Measure it the way `verify-defender` does: whole seasons through the real match sheet, and the winners' positions across many seasons should look like a spread, not a strikers' list. *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 22): Player of the Season goes to whoever stood furthest above his own position's field: each line's regulars (keepers, defenders, midfielders, forwards) give a mean and a spread, and a player's standing is how far above his line's mean he is, in its spreads. Measured over 60 whole seasons by `verify-defender`, winners by line (GK · DEF · MID · FWD): before, 0 · 0 · 31 · 29; now 3 · 8 · 15 · 34. Every line can win it, attackers still ahead as they are in football. The score on the card is still the real one; only the ranking changed. `verify-defender` now fails if forwards take more than two thirds or fewer than three lines ever win; `verify-awards` checks the winner is his line's best.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026.)*
*Added 24 September 2026, fixes from the batch 12 playtest (to confirm):*

~~**P8-156 · Missing crests on the knockout rows and the bracket preview.**~~ *(Fixed 24 September 2026: every tie drawn through `KoTieVM` lost its club ids, so the rows fell back to drawn initials ("LF", "CF"); the ids now go through all four adapters. The bracket preview only ever drew a nation's flag, so every club was a bare name; it uses the kit's `ClubName`, crest or flag.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-157 · UCL clubs' colours all black.**~~ *(Fixed 24 September 2026, in the data: 57 of the Champions League copies of clubs carried the scrapers' slate placeholder (#1E293B), which reads as black; only the match sheet's own lookup (P8-49) knew to replace it. The placeholder is now replaced by the same club's real colours in the bundled database itself (53 fixed; 5 clubs have no real colours anywhere), a `COLOUR_FIX` step in `build-db.ts` keeps it that way, and the database version is 14 so phones copy the fixed file.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-158 · Club names cut short ("FC" for FC Metz).**~~ *(Fixed 24 September 2026, to confirm: the same cause as Awards Night's "AWARDS". The super face is a true italic, and Android measures a line without the italic's lean past the last letter, so where a title is sized to its own measured width the drawn text is a hair wider than its box: Android wraps the last word onto a line the box then cuts off. Every super now has room for the lean on Android (`KitText`).)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-159 · Leg 2 went straight to penalties.**~~ *(Fixed 24 September 2026: the classic knockouts had their own copy of the live periods, which only added extra time when someone scored in it, so a goalless extra time vanished. The shared builder already had the fix; the copy is gone.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-160 · The shootout gave itself away.**~~ *(Fixed 24 September 2026: the rows drew a slot for every kick the shootout would have, so you knew from the start whether it went to sudden death. Now five regulation slots, and a sudden-death kick only once it's taken; and each kick names its taker as it's taken ("Kane (Arsenal) scores").)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-161 · The Champions League had no team of the matchday.**~~ *(Fixed 24 September 2026: the team of the matchday is one shared component now, `RoundTeam` (computed once per round, after the frame, inside `SafeSection`), under the results in the league season, the Champions League league phase, and the full path's domestic season and league phase.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

*Added 25 September 2026, from the batch 15 playtest:*

~~**P8-162 · The World Cup result puts your matches first.**~~ *(Done 25 September 2026: a Your matches section right under the figures, your last match on top — the knockouts newest first, then your group — each opening its sheet. Only the group games were listed, further down.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 25 September 2026. *(The maintainer, 25 September 2026: the rows don't say which round each was. *(Fixed 25 September 2026: each row carries its round above the score — ROUND OF 16, GROUP B · MD 2 — through a `round` line on the shared result row, which any list that mixes rounds can use.)*)*)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

~~**P8-163 · The spin is broken for the World Cup, and needs a wow upgrade.**~~ The World Cup spin shows neither the country's colours nor its flag. For every mode: the full crest (or flag) as the reel item's background, coloured from the picture itself rather than a stored colour; and more to the spin itself, another wow factor. (Centralisation F-23.) *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 23). **Every reel item wears its mark:** a club's card is its crest's colour with the crest big across it, bleeding off the edge; a nation's card is its flag, full bleed; the name sits on a small ink label so it reads on any colour, with the mark's second colour as a band along the foot. **The colours are read off the pictures themselves:** `scripts/mark-colours.ts` buckets every opaque pixel of all 922 crests and 306 flags, weighted by how colourful each colour is (so a crest's red beats the white it sits on, and a black-and-white crest stays black and white), and writes `src/lib/markColours.ts`; `markColoursOf` in `brand.ts` is the one reader. So World Cup nations have colours at last, and the scrapers' slate placeholder never shows. **The wow:** the card passing the marker swells (a lens), and the marker has a flapper that kicks over as each card goes by and springs back, fast at first, then one by one as the reel brakes, like a prize wheel's. Both run on the UI thread off the reel's one translate; reduced motion keeps neither. The reel's edges strobe in each mark's own colour now too.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026, with one note: a nation's card mixed the flag with its colours banded on top, and "looks awful"; it should be the flag alone, as on the draft. *(Fixed 28 September 2026: a nation's reel card is its flag, full bleed, with the name on its ink label and nothing else; the crest colours stay for clubs.)*)*

**P8-164 · The globe reveal, redone from scratch.** It looks good but lags badly and the country outlines are hard to see. Rebuild it; and instead of orange, the chosen country wears its flag. *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 23), rebuilt from scratch. **Why it lagged:** every frame, about 30 a second, React re-rendered the whole SVG, about 180 country paths, each projected afresh in JavaScript with its trigonometry on the JS thread. **Now:** every outline is turned into points on the unit sphere once, when the file loads, so a frame is a rotation (a few multiplications a point); all the land is one path; and the frames run on the UI thread through Reanimated's animated props, with no React render per frame (on the web Reanimated sets the path directly). A point behind the globe is pulled onto its edge instead of breaking the outline, so every country stays one closed shape. **Visible outlines:** light land on a dark sea, with dark borders between countries. **The chosen country wears its flag** once it lands, laid over the box it covers, in place of orange; the ring stays the accent. Reduced motion lands it at once. The About page's endless globe is on the same pieces. The old helper (`globe-geo.ts`) is gone. To measure on the phone in Phase 9.)* *(The maintainer, 28 September 2026: it looks and runs smoothly, but the borders look a little weird; proposal: after the spin, zoom slowly into the country, its proper outline with the flag inside, and the countries around it properly drawn but in a fog, less colourful, so all the attention is on your country. *(Done 28 September 2026: after the spin the globe zooms in on the country (1.5 s, easing in and out, finishing centred on it) until it spans about a third of the view (never more than 16×, so a tiny country stays a shape); the land and its borders fade into a fog (grey on the dark sea), the grid and the ring go, and the country rises out of it with an accent outline, then its flag. The settled frame is drawn once from every point of every outline, so the borders are crisp. All of it runs off one zoom value on the UI thread. The reveal's text arrives when the zoom has settled.)*)*

**P8-165 · The pundits, redone for every tournament.** The idea is right, the execution lacks. *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 23). **Their tournament, played out:** each pundit plays the whole cup out once from the real draw — the World Cup's twelve groups, the Champions League's league-phase fixtures — leaning heavily on the side they rate higher (55% between equals, about 80% at a six-point gap, never over 90%; a draw takes half the rest in a group game), then builds the knockouts from *their* tables the way the game builds the real ones: the top two and the eight best thirds into a drawn round of 32; 9th–24th into the play-off and the top eight drawn against its winners. `src/engine/cup-calls.ts`, pure and seeded. **On the result screens** (World Cup, Champions League, the full path): a rail of the panel together and all twelve pundits, each card with their champion and their score; a switch between **their** tournament and **what happened**, drawn with the same group wall or league table and the same bracket, so the two sit in one place; their bracket marks each side they sent through RIGHT if it really got that far, WRONG if not. **Points included:** their group and league-phase tables carry their points and goal difference. **Scored:** places exactly right, sides sent through that really went that far, and the champion. **Twelve versions:** each pundit's own ratings (kept on the panel now) and seed; the pundits screen and the result screens pick a pundit with the same shared rail (`PunditRail`). It replaces P8-56's list of real ties with who they'd have backed. `verify-cup-calls` checks the tables (games, points, goal difference), the bracket (every winner in the next round, in order; the champion won their final), the RIGHT/WRONG marks against how far each side really got, the score, determinism, and the lean: over 400 seasons the side they rated higher won **77%** of their 12,400 knockout ties, and every one of 60 panels had more than one champion among its twelve pundits.)* *(The maintainer, 28 September 2026: it works after the run, "great one", but it can't be seen on the pundits screen, before the simulation; and the long "pundits, checked" table for the World Cup (and the Champions League) "is stupid and not worth it". *(Fixed 28 September 2026. The groups and the league-phase fixtures are drawn inside the simulation, after the pundits have spoken, so **each pundit now makes their own draw**, the way the real one is made: the field in four pots by their ratings, a World Cup group one side from each pot, a Champions League side two opponents from each pot (every side eight games). **The pundits screen** shows the chosen view's whole tournament under their table: groups or league phase with points, then their bracket and champion. **The result screens replay exactly that tournament** against what happened, scored on what can be compared: of the sides they put through to the knockouts, how many really got there; in the Champions League, the league-phase places exactly right; the sides they sent through a round that really got that far; the champion. **The checked table is gone**, from all three result screens, with its engine. `verify-cup-calls`: the pots, every side's two opponents from each pot, the tables, the bracket, the marks, the score, and that the pundits screen and the result screen show the same tournament.)*)*

- **The bracket preview they never made:** the panel predicts the whole tournament, groups included — one simulation played out, heavily skewed towards the better side, so it reads as a prediction.
- **On the result screens:** switch between what happened and the pundits' view of it, so the two can be compared, points included. Today it's bland.
- **Twelve versions:** each pundit's own, as on the pundits screen (P8-56).
- The maintainer has screenshots of the current state for this.

The full design pass of both modes is still to do.)* *(**The design pass, done 28 September 2026** (batch 23): each mode has a look of its own on its season screens, not only its colours (`src/components/kit/modeLook.tsx`, one provider per screen). **Chaos is knocked about:** headings a little askew with a strip of ripped hazard tape, the league table and the scoreline card a degree off true, a hazard edge down the table; every figure stays level. **Cursed is a bad signal:** faint purple scanlines over the table and the card, and now and then a heading glitches into a purple ghost of itself for a blink (rare and short, P8-145's rule). ~~**P8-170 · The golden glove winner wasn't in the team of the season.**~~ The maintainer, 25 September 2026: the golden glove winner wasn't in the team of the season. They're picked by different measures (`src/engine/awards.ts`, as of this date): the golden glove goes to the keeper with the most clean sheets (saves break a tie), and the team of the season picks by each player's season score. So a keeper behind a good defence can win the glove with ordinary ratings. Decide which should give way: the glove could weigh the keeper's own play, or the team could take the glove winner in goal, or the page could say why they differ. *(Done 27 September 2026 (batch 23), decided by measurement. `verify-defender` over 60 seasons: the glove winner was the team's keeper in only **14**; in 20 he was only the sixth-best keeper or worse by season score, and the season score's best keeper was usually five or more clean sheets behind the leader. **The reason:** clean sheets belong to the whole defence, so they go to the keeper behind the best one, while a keeper's season score is carried by saves, ratings and how hard his club had it, which favour the busy keeper at a weak side. Both fair; the night can't crown one keeper and pick another. **So the golden glove keeps its real meaning (most clean sheets), and its winner keeps goal in the team of the season;** the other keepers can still make the bench. A line under the team says how it's picked, on both awards screens. Now 60 of 60, and `verify-defender` fails if it isn't.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

Ids written to the seeds, the crest scraper fetched the six, and the crest map has every club in every seed: 922 entries. **On the pages:** the awards' shirts carry the club's crest (a nation's flag at the World Cup) beside its code; the player page has his club's crest by its name; the club page's header uses the same mark, so a nation's page wears its flag.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 28 September 2026.)*

**P8-172 · The Champions League researched again, and the Europa and Conference Leagues.** The maintainer, 26 September 2026: a separate, long research pass into the UEFA Champions League, compared with how the game does it now and what it's missing (see P8-114's notes for what's already known: pots by rating, not coefficient; no matchday schedule rules). And now, not later: the **Europa League and Conference League** (UEFA's third tier), their formats (the Conference League's league phase is six matches from six pots of six, not the Champions League's shape), their access lists and qualifying, and how a full path through either could work, since in reality you reach them by missing the Champions League (a league finish, a domestic cup, or dropping out of Champions League qualifying). Research **how coefficients work** (club and association), and everything around them, including which domestic cups give European places and how a place passes down when a cup winner has already qualified (The Dugout's `docs/reference` already has the UEFA papers and an answer to that question). A research-then-build entry: the research comes first, as documents. *(Done 28 September 2026 (batches 23 and 24). **The research** is [`docs/europe/`](../europe/00-README.md), six documents: the Champions League against ours, the Europa and Conference Leagues, coefficients, domestic cups and the full path, from UEFA's archived papers and The Dugout's verified engine. **Two new modes, the Europa League and the Conference League,** each on its real 2025–26 league-phase field (72 clubs matched to their squads, their crests and colours; the bundled database is at version 15) and its real pots. The Europa League draws the Champions League's way; the Conference League draws six pots of six, one opponent from each, three at home and three away (Euler orientation), with the same country rule; the pundits draw it the same way. Then the Champions League's knockouts, names, logos and colours of their own (Europa orange, Conference green), boards on Ranks, achievements, and a score weighted 0.8 and 0.65 of the Champions League's round for round. `verify-draw` draws both real fields under the full rules. **To apply:** redeploy `submit-run`, make a new build.)*

**P8-173 · Domestic cups.** The maintainer, 26 September 2026: the FA Cup, the DFB-Pokal and the rest. Two ways to do it, to decide: fold the cup into the league mode so a run is a literal full season (league and cup together), or make cups a mode of their own. *(Done 28 September 2026 (batch 24), **folded in** ([`docs/europe/04`](../europe/04-DOMESTIC-CUPS.md) §4): every league run plays its league's cup, the FA Cup, the Copa del Rey, the Coppa Italia, the DFB-Pokal or the Coupe de France (`src/engine/domestic-cup.ts`). The top flight only, said on screen: sixteen clubs, the lowest-rated playing a first round when there are more; an open draw each round from the run's seed; one match with extra time and penalties. The rounds fall between matchdays, the first a sixth of the way in, the final after the last matchday. **In the season:** a Cup tab with every round played, your tie first, and your tie under your match card the matchday its round is played. **On the verdict:** the cup's section, your ties and the final, and **the Double** when you win both. Saved with the run. Two feats, **Cup winners** and **The Double**. The score still comes from the league. `verify-cup` plays 1,020 cups over league sizes 8 to 24. Not done: the cup ties' scorers and match sheets (a scoreline only), and the lower divisions.)*

*Added 26 September 2026:*

~~**P8-174 · Links that open the app directly (the credentials step).**~~ P8-121's run links open the app by way of a small web page that hands the link over (`pom://`). For an `https://…/r/<id>` link to open the app straight from a chat, with no page in between, the phone has to trust that the website and the app belong together: **Android App Links** (and iOS universal links, if there's ever an iOS build). That trust is a file on the website, `/.well-known/assetlinks.json`, that names the app's package and **the SHA-256 fingerprint of the certificate the app is signed with**, plus an intent filter in `app.json` with `autoVerify` (which needs a new build). The fingerprint is the part only the maintainer can get: `eas credentials` → Android → the production build's keystore → **SHA-256 Fingerprint**. With it: add the file (served from `public/.well-known/`, with Vercel sending it as JSON), add the intent filter for `/r/*`, make a new build, and check with Google's Statement List tester. Soon, so it isn't forgotten. *(Started 27 September 2026: the maintainer read the fingerprint from `eas credentials` (the Default build credentials, which sign every EAS build of this app). Set up: `public/.well-known/assetlinks.json` (the package and a placeholder for the SHA-256 fingerprint; the web export copies it to the site), Vercel serving it as JSON, and the intent filter in `app.json` (`https://perfection-or-misery.vercel.app/r/…`, `autoVerify`). **Left for the maintainer:** paste the fingerprint into the file, deploy the web, make a new Android build, and check with Google's Statement List tester. If the app is ever on Google Play with Play App Signing, Google signs it with its own key: that fingerprint (Play Console → App integrity) goes in the same list.)* *(Finished 27 September 2026: the maintainer pasted the fingerprint, deployed the web and made a new Android build.)* *(Confirmed working by the maintainer, 27 September 2026: "the links actually work fantastically".)*

**P8-179 · Analytics and Speed Insights on the web.** The maintainer, 27 September 2026: add Vercel's analytics, and the other measurements worth having, so they can be read in the Vercel dashboard, the landing page included. From Vercel's list of drop-in packages, the two that fit: **Web Analytics** (`@vercel/analytics`: visits, page views, where visitors come from, their devices; no cookies) and **Speed Insights** (`@vercel/speed-insights`: the Core Web Vitals — how fast the page shows, how quickly it answers a tap, whether it jumps about — measured on real visitors' devices). Not taken: the AI SDK, storage, queues, edge config and the platform SDKs, which answer needs the game doesn't have; custom events (a run finished, a mode picked) are Pro-only, so they wait until then. On the Hobby plan Web Analytics counts 50,000 events a month, shared across every project on the account, and keeps one month of history; past the allowance, collection pauses rather than charging (Vercel's pricing page, read 27 September 2026). Both packages are web only: the phone app measures nothing. *(Done 27 September 2026: one component, `WebInsights`, mounted once in the root layout, so every page of the web build is measured, the landing page (`/`) included; the native build gets an empty twin and bundles neither package. Pages are grouped by route (`/u/[id]` is one page, not one per player) and the query string is never sent (`/game/result?runId=…` goes as `/game/result`). The v2 packages' build-time script address reaches Expo through the build command (see `docs/Web & Desktop Deployment.md`). **The privacy page is updated:** it said "no analytics", which is no longer true; it now says what the website measures and what it doesn't, and that the phone app sends none of it (and, fixed on the way, that a run being drafted is kept on the device, P8-149). **To turn it on:** the Vercel project's Analytics and Speed Insights tabs, Enable on each, then deploy. When the landing page is built (Phase 10) it carries the same two. **Everything about it** — turning it on, the free plan's limits, reading the numbers from the CLI, and how the Phase 8.5 audit and the Phase 10 landing page use it — is in [`docs/Web Analytics & Speed Insights.md`](<../Web%20Analytics%20%26%20Speed%20Insights.md>).)*

Every one of the 182 nationalities in the database now finds its flag (`flagForNationality`, checked by `verify-nationality`), the slot's small corner flag included. **More to the card:** the flag is full bleed, a little oversized and tilted, like a flag waved behind him; a shade rises from the foot so his name reads on any flag instead of a flat black band; his position chip and the card's foot wear his line's colour (keeper, defence, midfield, attack), so a grid of sixteen reads by shape at a glance; the rating on a cotton chip, ICON in gold; picked, the foot goes orange.)*

One club at a time. **Your tag** is your club's: the ID tag shows nothing until you join, then the tag, in Ranks, on runs and on your profile. **The club chat** is for members only (row-level security), live through Supabase Realtime, with deleting your own messages. Screens: Clubs from You, a club's page, its chat. **To apply:** run `supabase/clubs.sql`; until then Clubs says it's waiting.)*

## Phase 8.5 · The audit (added 24 September 2026)

### Part zero · The playtest notes of 29 September 2026 (done first)

The maintainer's playtest of Phase 8's last batches. **These are done first in Phase 8.5, before the audit itself starts.** Notes only for now: each is the maintainer's point, then what the code shows today (read 29 September 2026) where it was checked. The research that follows is in [`../centralisation/`](../centralisation/00-README.md) (phase two, from `08-`), [`../europe/`](../europe/00-README.md) (from `06-`) and [`../website/`](../website/00-README.md).

**P8.5-01 · The continue button, on some screens.** *(Answered 1 Oct, see P8.5-01 "Responsive buttons" below, built in Wave A; the waiting look refined in Phase 9.)* The note reads "for some screens the continue button basically" and stops there. A centralisation note: ask the maintainer what he saw before acting (the likeliest reading is that the step's forward plate sits or reads differently from screen to screen; the screen walk in [`../centralisation/06-SCREEN-WALK.md`](../centralisation/06-SCREEN-WALK.md) lists every step's forward plate).

**P8.5-02 · Profile changes don't show on the You page.** The profile itself is "a lot better, already liking it". *Today:* the You tab draws only the look's colour band from your public profile (`LookBand` in `app/(tabs)/profile.tsx`); the frame, the theme, the banner picture, the status line, the about line and the season badges you set in the editor only appear on your public page (`app/u/[id].tsx`, `ProfileCard`). The You page should wear the same card. *(Done 1 Oct: signed in, You wears the same `ProfileCard` as your public page; a tap opens the editor. A guest keeps the ID tag.)*

**P8.5-03 · Pundits.** *(Answered 1 Oct below: it meant P8.5-15.)* Written as one word in the notes; read with P8.5-15 (the full path has no pundits) and ask if it meant more.

**P8.5-04 · The share picture should say it's your run.** The shared picture carries the run, but not you, so whoever receives it can't tell whose run it is. *Today:* the verdict card's owner line (P8-89) shows only on a saved run opened again, and says YOUR RUN rather than your name; the run you've just played has none (`VerdictBlock`, `ownerId` left out). The card should carry your name, picture and club tag every time it's shared. *(Done 1 Oct: the card itself (the part that's shared) carries the owner's picture, "NAME'S RUN" and club tag, the run you've just played included; `useRunOwner` reads the club too.)*

**P8.5-05 · Clubs: deleting, handing over, leaving.** You can't delete a club (it closes only when nobody is left in it), you can't hand the ownership to someone else, and an owner leaving should be able to choose who takes over. *Today:* `supabase/clubs.sql` has `create_club`, `join_club`, `leave_club` (ownership passes to the longest-standing member; an empty club closes), `remove_from_club` and `update_club`; there's no delete and no transfer. *(Done 1 Oct, needs `supabase/clubs-2.sql`: `delete_club`, `transfer_club`, `leave_club(p_heir)`; the club page's owner sections Hand it over and Close the club.)*

**P8.5-06 · The club chat.** It looks bland, "kind of awful", and while you type you can't see the field: the keyboard covers it. It should rise with the keyboard the way WhatsApp's and Instagram's do. *Today:* `app/club/chat.tsx` uses `KeyboardAvoidingView` with `behavior` set only on iOS (`undefined` on Android), so on Android nothing moves the field above the keyboard. *(Done 1 Oct: the field lifts with the keyboard on Android (`padding` on both; SDK 54 is edge to edge, so the window no longer resizes); yours a solid orange bubble, the Kit's offset block behind each, a square send key.)*

**P8.5-07 · Clubs in the bottom navigation.** The Clubs entry moves from You to the tab bar on the base screen, so it's one tap away. *(Done 1 Oct: Clubs is the fourth tab (`app/(tabs)/clubs.tsx`, still /clubs); the row on You went.)*

**P8.5-08 · Invite-only clubs and passwords.** A club can be invite-only or have a password, so you can make one just for your own team; a club with a password says so in the search results (a lock). *(Done 1 Oct, needs `supabase/clubs-2.sql`: Who can join (anyone, invite only, password); the password kept as a bcrypt hash in `club_secrets`, a table nobody can read; invite by username; invites on the Clubs tab; a lock and the way in on search results.)*

**P8.5-09 · A clubs leaderboard.** The same as Ranks, but for clubs: no seasons, and a club's score is the total of all its members' runs added together. *(Done 1 Oct, needs `supabase/clubs-2.sql`: Ranks → Clubs, every member's runs added together by `club_board`, your club outlined.)*

**P8.5-10 · A swearing filter for clubs, with a switch.** A list of words, on by default and switchable per club (close friends can turn it off). A message isn't refused: the word is replaced. **Decided: a funny word, not #####** ("fuck" becomes "fiddlesticks", and so on for the rest of the list), and now and then a custom line in the club's own voice, like "Come on, seriously?" and "No need for that around here, mate.", with more written for it. *(Done 1 Oct, needs `supabase/clubs-2.sql`: a trigger swaps each listed word as the message is written, so no app gets round it; the owner's Clean language switch; a cleaned message gets a line in the club's voice (ten written). The list is the table `club_swears`, started with 26 English words; the maintainer's list and a researched one (English and Slovak) are still to add, as rows, no app build.)*

**P8.5-11 · The draft's player cards: no tilt, the whole card is the flag.** The cards are "nice, I like it", but the flag should be the whole background, not a small tilted thing. *Today:* the flag is drawn oversized, rotated −5°, under a shade that rises to 92% ink at the foot (P8-180), so on a short card much of it is covered. *(Done 1 Oct: the flag fills the card, level; the shade starts halfway down, so the top half is all flag.)*

**P8.5-12 · The globe.** The new animation is better ("for other countries, wow").
- **Put it on the About page** too, which still shows the plain spinning globe (`SpinningGlobe`).
- **France is too small.** *Measured:* France's outline in `assets/geo/countries-110m.geo.json` includes French Guiana (longitudes −54.5 to 9.6), and the zoom's centre (`centroidOf`) and reach (`reachOf`) in `GlobeReveal.tsx` take every polygon, so the camera centres somewhere over the Atlantic and zooms out to take in South America. The zoom should frame the country's main landmass. *(Done 1 Oct: centre and reach come from the largest polygon, `mainFeature`; France now centres at about 3.3° E, 47.0° N.)*
- **The black box behind it.** The globe sits in a dark panel on the cotton page. Light and dark modes don't exist yet; **for Phase 8.5: every component needs a light and a dark variant** (see the audit's design lens). *(Done 1 Oct: the panel's own nylon fill is gone; the globe is a dark sphere on either ground.)*

**P8.5-13 · Europe's seasons: crests and the cups.** The holders' and the cup winners' rows don't use crests, and you can't see the cups themselves: show each cup as its bracket instead of a list. (P8-52's ceremony.) *(Done 1 Oct: the holders' and cup winners' rows carry crests (`MarkRow`); every cup opens as its bracket (`openCupBracket`, `cupToColumns` in `src/lib/bracket.ts`, a first round's byes in its column so it halves). The league run's Cup tab has the bracket too.)*

**P8.5-14 · After qualifying, the rest of Europe.** Once qualifying is over you can't look at the other competitions' qualifying, and it's hard to see who else got into the league phases. Under the INTO THE CHAMPIONS LEAGUE stamp (and its Europa and Conference League versions), a quick list of who else got in, for all three competitions. *(Done 1 Oct: under the stamp, "The rest of Europe": a switch between the three, each league phase's 36 with DIRECT or VIA its round, then that competition's whole qualifying ladder.)*

**P8.5-15 · The full path's pundits.** "Still unsure where the pundits are for the full path." *Today:* there are none. The full path's draw goes straight to the domestic season ("Start your league season" in `app/game/placement.tsx`); the pundits screen is only reached from the other modes' draws. *(Done 1 Oct: the full path's pre-season is "Hear the pundits"; they call your domestic league on the shared pundits screen (`src/lib/punditsHandoff.ts`), "Prove them wrong" starts the season, and the domestic result says what they had you and how you did. Kept off the run's `predictionSeed`, which the result checks against the league phase.)*

**P8.5-16 · The full path's result: every competition.** On the result screen you should be able to switch to the other two competitions and see how they finished. *(Done 1 Oct: the other two competitions are played out headless at the end of a run (`otherCompetitions`, saved on the result as `others`); the result's "The rest of Europe" switches between them: winner, bracket, league phase.)*

**P8.5-17 · The draw's bowl.** Better, and the core is nice, but still a little broken: the balls' spinning. *(Done 1 Oct: the wheel is gone; the capsules lie in a pile in the bowl and each jostles on its own loop, one clock, seamless.)*

**P8.5-18 · The Leagues button in the knockouts goes.** The full path's knockout screen still has a Leagues button in its header; remove it. *(Done 1 Oct.)*

**P8.5-19 · The stakes list cuts off the path.** From the maintainer's screenshot (Romania, association 22): the Europa and Conference League rows run out of room ("Conference League, Second Qualifying Round…"), and the Champions League's row doesn't name its competition while the others do. *Today:* `PositionStakes` in `src/components/CustomUclViewers.tsx` puts it all on one line. *(Done 1 Oct: two lines a row, the competition named on every one, the round and path under it.)*

**P8.5-20 · Domestic cups for every nation, not only the top five.** The maintainer, 29 September 2026: "I do think as well as for domestic cups making them for all nations." Researched in [`../europe/06-CUPS-FOR-EVERY-NATION.md`](../europe/06-CUPS-FOR-EVERY-NATION.md): the game plays every association's cup on the full path already, but only ten have their real name, none is shown, and only a league run's cup is played through the season. The plan: one table of all 55 names, every cup kept and shown as a bracket, your own cup played through your season, two-legged semi-finals where they're real. *(Done 1 Oct, steps 1 to 4 of the plan: `NATIONAL_CUPS`, 53 associations named ("{country} Cup" in the public build), `CUP_NAME`/`CUP_BY_COUNTRY` gone; every cup kept on the run and shown as a bracket (the saved run keeps winners only); your own cup played through your season with a Cup tab, the played one deciding the cup's place; two-legged semi-finals for the four cups checked for 2025–26 (Copa del Rey, Coppa Italia, Taça de Portugal, Belgian Cup). Checks: `verify-europe-path`, `verify-cup`. Not done: seeds and sheets for cup matches (§4.2 item 5).)*

**P8.5-21 · The full path as a European path, with achievements and tiers.** The maintainer, 29 September 2026: the full path should let you go into anything, needs achievements beyond easy, medium and hard, since you can win the Champions League, the Europa League or the Conference League, and asks how someone hunting the Conference League on hard gets there ("do they just have to get lucky?"). Researched and measured in [`../europe/07-THE-EUROPEAN-PATH.md`](../europe/07-THE-EUROPEAN-PATH.md): yes, today it is luck twice over (about one run in 240 ends in a Conference League win at team OVR 86 on hard); the club the draw picks barely steers it; the plan is a name, a nine-patch achievements grid (three competitions, three difficulties), six feats, a competition filter on Ranks, and an optional "your target" choice that makes your finish decide how deep you enter rather than which competition. *(Done 1 Oct: "European Full Path" (tag EUROPE); the achievements grid, three trophies by four difficulties, each with an AIMED line; the Ranks Competition and Hunting filters (by the tier's prefix and `difficulty_meta->>target`, no migration); hunting (option B) as `aimAt` in `src/engine/europe-path.ts`, never a drop, every league phase still 36; the Europa League's qualifying exits as tiers; `TARGET_MULTIPLIER` 0.9 / 0.85 / 0.7; the six feats, "The long way" at four ties (measured: the most a season can have you play, so the plan's five could never be earned); `scripts/measure-europe.ts`. Needs `submit-run` redeployed.)*

**P8.5-22 · The research written on 29 September 2026.** *(Carried to Wave G and closed there: the re-score, centralisation phase two and the carry-forward's items; the carry-forward's Phase 9 items are in Phase 9's note.)* Centralisation re-audited ([`../centralisation/08-RE-AUDIT.md`](../centralisation/08-RE-AUDIT.md), [`09-NEW-ITEMS.md`](../centralisation/09-NEW-ITEMS.md), [`10-PHASE-TWO-REVISED.md`](../centralisation/10-PHASE-TWO-REVISED.md): the playtest notes go first); this whole overhaul set re-read against the app ([`13-CARRY-FORWARD.md`](13-CARRY-FORWARD.md): what's stale, what the light-and-dark, "corporate" and new-logo notes reopen, and what Phases 9 and 10 still owe from it); the website planned ([`../website/`](../website/00-README.md)). The first act of Phase 8.5 proper is still to re-score [`01-CRITIQUE.md`](01-CRITIQUE.md)'s ten heuristics.

**The release decisions of 29 September 2026.** 0.9.0 is built. **1.0.0 = Phases 8.5, 9 and 10 plus finishing up**, released under a football name (*First Touch*, *The Academy*, or another; the maintainer picks) with a welcome update. **Security and performance are day one; features can wait.** The app-wide research is in [`../release/`](../release/00-README.md); the website's 31 answers in [`../website/05-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`](../website/05-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md). The items below come early in Phase 8.5, **P8.5-32 (the data) first, then injection.**

**P8.5-33 · Our own rating colours, and nothing else borrowed.** *(Done 1 Oct, to playtest: the bands the maintainer set, 9+ magenta #F264FF, 8 volt, 7 mint #62D39A, 6.5 slate #9FB0C2, 6 amber #E0A43A, 5 the logo's red, under 5 violet #8E4FD6; `RATING_BANDS` in `src/theme.ts`. The sweep: square, thicker stat bars; the breaks are a band with the score ("Half-time · 1–0", "After 90", "After extra time") instead of a pill between hairlines; added time is "Board up · +4"; the feed's headings are ours (Goal, Penalty, scored, Booked, Sent off, Change, Chalked off); the red wash on a sending-off is gone (red badge and heading instead); About and the guide no longer say "FotMob-style".)* The maintainer, 29 September 2026: "even for the stupid ratings we need to change it so nothing is actually from the app." *Today:* `ratingColor` in `src/theme.ts:240` uses SofaScore's six bands and its exact colours (`#374DF5`, `#00ADC4`, `#00C424`, `#D9AF00`, `#ED7E07`, `#DC0C00`), taken from SofaScore's published scale image, as its own comment says (P8-44). Redesign: PoM's own bands and colours from the Kit Drop palette and the logo's ladder (volt for Perfection down to the logo's red for Misery), checked for contrast in dark and light mode, with `ratingInk` and every rating chip following. The same pass sweeps the other borrowings the code names: the "FotMob's way" match-sheet pieces (`src/components/MatchStatsParts.tsx`, `src/engine/commentary.ts`) keep the general idea (a timeline, a feed) but get PoM's own wording, layout details and colours, so no screen reads as a copy of another app.

The halving of the two screens isn't met; one shared stage loop is carried as step 4b. Checks S8-1 to S8-8. *Closed 5 Oct:* the re-score is **38/40** ([`../audit-2026-10/05-UI-RESCORE.md`](../audit-2026-10/05-UI-RESCORE.md) §4; the match sheet holds aesthetic at 3), and the recount closes 87 of 90 centralisation items, with C-15's remainder, N-14 and N-16 handed over ([`../centralisation/12-PHASE-TWO-FINAL.md`](../centralisation/12-PHASE-TWO-FINAL.md) §7). **Phase 8.5 is closed.** |

Centralisation is Wave G (the audit proper) plus its phase two; **Phase 9 starts only once centralisation is finished** (the maintainer, 1 Oct 2026). From Wave D on, what can't be checked on the web waits for **Phase 9.5** (after Phase 9), listed in [`../PHASE-9.5-CHECKLIST.md`](../PHASE-9.5-CHECKLIST.md).

**P8.5-01 · Responsive buttons (defined 1 Oct).** The maintainer: "find them yourself, also for the continue button problem we need responsive buttons everywhere, aka when you click on it and it is waiting for a request or even if it is not, you need to see the text change to waiting or something while also it darkening a little as if you would tap it and kept holding it." *(Built 1 Oct in the Kit's `Plate`, all 141 of them: a tap holds the plate in, darkens it, says "Waiting…" and runs the action a frame later, so a continue that starts heavy work no longer freezes before the press shows; a promise holds it until it settles; no second tap while waiting. Other tappable surfaces (rows, cards) keep their pressed look.)*

**P8.5-03 · Pundits (answered 1 Oct).** It meant only P8.5-15: the full path has no pundits. *(Done with P8.5-15, 1 Oct.)* Checked: the full path's draw goes straight to the domestic season ("Start your league season", `app/game/placement.tsx`); the pundits screen is reached only from the other modes' draws.

**P8.5-34 · The About globe (added 1 Oct).** "In the about section the globe is really weak and awful (the borders are not sharp…)". The new behaviour: it spins, and when Slovakia comes round it slowly zooms until the whole country fills the view, with a small dot where the game was built (north of Bratislava, between Stupava, Malacky, Pezinok and Senec: about 48.32° N, 17.18° E); it holds there, then zooms out and spins on, round again. With sharp borders. *(Done 1 Oct: one clock drives spin, zoom-in (2.4 s), hold (3.2 s) and zoom-out over a 44 s cycle; the land is drawn from every point at one decimal, so the borders are sharp; an orange dot with a pulse marks 48.32° N, 17.18° E; with reduced motion it holds on Slovakia.)*

**P8.5-32 · Replace the scraped data (first of all).** *(Done 30 Sept – 1 Oct, wave 0: open data from Wikipedia and Wikidata.)* Transfermarkt's terms (§11.1) forbid scraping; the EU database right and the GDPR add to it; the public web build already serves the scraped database. No more scrape runs; a new database from public-domain sources (Wikidata, openfootball) with a PoM rating built from facts, calibrated against today's; the web build switched first; crests from Transfermarkt's image host removed; a privacy section for player data. Transfermarkt gets a forward-looking licensing enquiry. [`../release/06-OUR-OWN-DATA.md`](../release/06-OUR-OWN-DATA.md), [`../release/07-ASKING-TRANSFERMARKT.md`](../release/07-ASKING-TRANSFERMARKT.md).

**P8.5-23 · Injection fixes (first).** *(Done 1 Oct: I1 club search as two `.ilike()` filters with LIKE wildcards escaped; every filter-string id through `uuid()` (`src/lib/uuid.ts`), the profile link's id checked first; I3 a `?` parameter; delete-account checks its id; `scripts/verify-no-string-queries.ts` scans app/, src/, supabase/ and fails if one comes back (proven by reintroducing I3). The edge function needs redeploying.)* Three queries built from text: the club search's filter string (`src/db/queries/clubs.ts:74`), the friend lookup's filter from a URL id (`src/lib/friends.ts:130`), and one local SQLite query (`src/db/queries/seasons.ts:224`); plus a UUID check on every other filter string and a scan that fails if one comes back. [`../release/03-INJECTION.md`](../release/03-INJECTION.md).

**P8.5-24 · Offline.** *(Done 1 Oct: a queue on the phone (`src/lib/runQueue.ts`) with a `client_id` per run so a resend is never a duplicate and `played_at` kept, flushed on start, on reconnect and on return to the app; an offline strip and notices on every online screen; `supabase/runs-queue.sql`; `scripts/verify-run-queue.ts`.)* Today a run saved without a connection is retried only on its result screen and then lost. A save queue on the phone that sends runs when the connection returns (no duplicates, the played-at time kept), and an offline mode that changes the app around automatically (online-only features say so and step aside). The true last thing before Phase 9: an audit of the whole app, engine to UI. In the maintainer's words, the small things: *a player scores a hat-trick yet loses and doesn't even get an 8.5*; *does your team's strength match what you built*; *is it FUN to play?*

**Part one — look at everything through three lenses:**

- **Design choices.** Question every choice, the engine's as much as the UI's: why is it like that, and is there a better way? Nothing is exempt because it was already decided.
- **Centralisation.** "We use something once, we use it over and over, so nothing feels out of place." Every place the same thing is drawn or computed twice.
- **Fresh ideas.** What to expand, what to lean into.

**Part two — critique, then check the critique.** Criticise everything found. Then, only after, look at each criticism again with a clear head: is this valid, constructive criticism, or criticism for the sake of being asked for it? The engine choices especially. It includes how we test that things hold up — whether the verifiers measure what matters (the hat-trick in a defeat, strength matching the build), not only invariants.

Run it with the `deep-audit` skill (a complete audit), whose documents become the plan for what follows. Also carry in the P8 notes that point here.

*Added 25 September 2026:* the old match sheet still draws countries as emoji inside the text (`withFlag`, centralisation A-07) — part of this audit's centralisation work.

*Added 27 September 2026:* **the audit measures first, from the web's own numbers** (P8-179). Before any critique, snapshot Web Analytics (which pages people use, where they come from, on what device) and Speed Insights (how each page performs for real visitors) into the audit's documents, with dates and data-point counts, because the free plan keeps only a week of Speed Insights and a month of Analytics. Then rank the web work by use times slowness, split phone from computer, and name the reason before fixing. Speed Insights on the free plan shows only the overall score in the dashboard; whether `vercel metrics` gives the individual vitals is the first thing to try. None of it covers the phone app: those numbers are Phase 9's. The step by step, the CLI queries and the thresholds: [`docs/Web Analytics & Speed Insights.md`](<../Web%20Analytics%20%26%20Speed%20Insights.md>) §4.

*Added 28 September 2026:* one of the recent criticisms of the UI and the feel: the game feels "corporate", not like a football app; too colourless. Part of this audit's design lens: where the colour, the noise and the matchday feel should come from.

*Added 27 September 2026:* a responsiveness pass across the app (the maintainer: most screens still need it, since they break easily). Every screen at a narrow phone, a large phone, a tablet and the web's column and wide layouts; long names, big numbers and large system text; nothing cut off, overlapping or pushed off the edge. The titles cut off at the top (batch 17's screenshots) were this kind of fault.
