### The Wave C playtest notes (1 October 2026, evening)

The maintainer, after Wave C on the phone. All of it goes into Wave D.

**P8.5-44 · Moderation: bad words, names, reports and bans.** The 26 starter words are nowhere near enough: "there are some really bad racial words and other words, we have to look at it from every angle possible, both English and Slovak." What it has to cover:
- **Every kind of word:** swearing, slurs (racial and others), vulgar words for body parts, and terms with an awful meaning (paedophilia, transphobia and the like), in English and Slovak.
- **From a real list,** kept up by someone, not only words typed by hand; plus the maintainer's own list and what reports turn up.
- **Disguised spellings:** "f4ck" and the like are caught. **Words inside words** too: "Lafuckda" becomes "Lafudgeda".
- **Chat stays cheeky.** In club chat (where the filter can be switched off) a player who really wants to can still write around it: the filter makes them write it differently, and an ordinary player never notices it. A word is replaced, never refused, and the chat shows no telling-off ("double punishment, which is not needed": the club-voice lines under a cleaned message go).
- **Names are strict.** Usernames, club names and tags, and anything else public that can never carry one, refuse it outright.
- **The refusal speaks your language,** and isn't a translation: each language has its own lines, e.g. Slovak "A ty tu čo skúšaš, môj?", English its own.
- **Unsure cases go to a review inbox.** A name like "IslamHater2121" holds no listed word but is plainly wrong: it's flagged for review rather than let through or refused blind (a username status: active, pending review, rejected).
- **Reports:** from a player's or a club's page, a Report button and a reason. Reports land in the same inbox; a few reports on one name hide it until it's looked at.
- **Bans:** a way to force a name change, or ban an account.
The maintainer's research brief (pasted 1 Oct) suggests a local filter with leetspeak handling (glin-profanity, or bad-words extended), a hate/slur layer (OpenAI's free Moderation API, which has a hate category), a community-maintained slur list as data, a pending-review state, a reports table and a small admin page. **Decision for him:** an outside checker (the OpenAI API) means sending names and messages to a third party, which the privacy page has to say. The default if nobody decides: local lists and rules only, plus reports and review. *(Decided 1 Oct: local only.)*

*(Done 1 Oct, waiting for the playtest:*
- *All of it runs in the database (`supabase/moderation.sql`), so no app can get round it.*
- *The words: 1,384 of them, built by `scripts/build-moderation.ts` from LDNOOBW English and Czech (CC BY 4.0), cuss's surest words (MIT) and our own English and Slovak lists, with funny swaps (kurva → kukurica). Sources are in `scripts/moderation/SOURCES.md`.*
- *Disguises: digits read as any vowel (f4ck); stretched letters (fuuuck); separators in names; camelCase.*
- *Severe words are caught inside words ("Lafuckda" → "Lafudgeda"); an allowlist protects Scunthorpe and Picasso.*
- *Names (usernames, club names, tags and descriptions, status, pronouns, about, favourite player) are refused with a random line in the phone's language (English or Slovak, each its own lines). The rename screen checks before sign-in changes, so a refused name can't half-rename an account.*
- *Unsure names (a group plus a hostile word, a "maybe" word) are saved and flagged.*
- *Chat is swapped only, not across separators.*
- *Reports come from a player's or club's page and are collected with the unsure names. The inbox (Let it be, Take the name, Ban, Close) moved to the website on 1 Oct, the maintainer's call: `docs/website/02` §5a. Until then it runs from the SQL editor (`docs/MODERATION.md`), and the app has no moderator role.*
- *A ban stops runs, chat, clubs and reports; a taken name asks for a new one.*
- *Every real club and player name in the DB goes through the check: a downloaded word that would refuse one is demoted to review (22 of them: Dago, Haji, Pollock…). Four of 22,767 players are still refused, all by our own words, on purpose: Adrián Pica, Denis Suka, Neil Pace Cocks, Jizz Hornkamp.*
- *`scripts/verify-moderation.ts` runs all of it in PGlite.*
- *Not built: hiding a name after a few reports (only the inbox orders by report count).)*

**Phase order (the maintainer, 1 Oct):** centralisation is the audit proper (Wave G) plus doing what it finds (phase two). Phase 9 starts only after centralisation is finished.

## Phase 9 · Mobile performance and the Diagnostics screen (added 18 September 2026)

*Started 5 October 2026, after Phase 8.5 closed. Diagnostics first, since the rest of the phase is measured with it.* *Diagnostics step 1 done 5 Oct:* the budget contract, the recorder and one log for the whole app (every console call moved onto it, warnings and errors kept on the phone across a crash), a recovery screen for a screen that throws, and sign-out no longer wipes the device's storage (on the web it took the settings and runs waiting to go up with it). [`../diagnostics/06-IMPLEMENTATION.md`](../diagnostics/06-IMPLEMENTATION.md) step 1. Checks P9-1 to P9-3. *Step 2 done 5 Oct:* every budget is measured where it happens (the database, boot, every screen change, the draft, each matchday and skip, the brackets, the match sheets, the stats, saves and loads, the frames of the globes, the bracket, the Deep Match and the ceremony), with stalls pinned to the screen and the work in progress, memory every 10 s, and RUN STARTED / RUN ENDED and screen lines in the log. Checks P9-4 to P9-6. *Step 3 done 5 Oct:* the self-test (benchmarks, the engine fingerprint against a golden file Node writes, the match rules over 200 synthetic matches, the database, the backend), one copy of the match rules for the scripts and the phone, `ENGINE_VERSION` so changing what a seed produces is a decision, and a report of what a reload loses. Checks P9-7 and P9-8. *Step 4 done 6 Oct:* the Diagnostics screen and its log, a report that fits one chat message with anything personal blanked, share or copy, and the ways in (eight taps on About, an About row, Ctrl+Shift+D, the crash screen). Checks P9-9 to P9-12. *Step 5 built 6 Oct:* the tester under Diagnostics (developer builds only), build sizes weighed (`scripts/perf-size.ts`) into a new [`../PERF-LOG.md`](../PERF-LOG.md), and a leak fixed on the way (the self-test had put real club names into the public web bundle; caught by the first run of the legal-bundle check on a real export, now guarded). **Waiting on the maintainer:** the first report from the phone, which the rest of Phase 9 is measured against. Checks P9-13 and P9-14.

*Added 22 September 2026:* switching tabs during a simulation has a visible delay (the maintainer, on the phone). Profile it with the rest of this phase before touching it.
*Added 25 September 2026:* the friends screen takes a while to load its data, with nothing in between — it needs a loading state. Opening every tie in the pundits' tournament view lags the phone badly.
*Added 27 September 2026:* the Deep Match still draws its formation with an old version of the pitch view; move it to the shared one (the maintainer).
*Added 4 October 2026 (handed over by centralisation phase two, step 8):* **N-14**, the long lists (Ranks, Runs, the stats boards, the 36-club tables) still render every row, and only the club chat is virtualised: measure them with the rest of this phase ([`10-ADAPT-OPTIMIZE-A11Y.md`](10-ADAPT-OPTIMIZE-A11Y.md) §3.2). **N-16**, the globe twice (`GlobeReveal` on the draw, `SpinningGlobe` on About): one drawing for both, which the globe item below already plans.
*Added 1 October 2026 (the maintainer, after Wave B):*
- **The live match's own timing.** The phone's log already prints a line per live match, e.g. `[live] Thatguy XI v Omonia Nicosia: 24.1 s, 60 ms a minute` and `[live] Real Betis Balompié v Thatguy XI: 14.9 s, 60 ms a minute`: the same 60 ms a match-minute, but 24 s against 15 s for the match. Find where the extra nine seconds go (pauses on moments, extra time, the reveal) before calling it slow.
- **Loading you can see, YouTube's way.** Data loads behind "ghost" outlines of the content (skeleton rows the shape of what's coming), so a load feels like progress, not a blank.
- **§1 · Logs, everywhere.** An absurd amount of logging, on purpose: every action and everything that happens, with its time and its cost. Cost means time **and** memory, what's loaded and what isn't, what comes back and how fast. Logs come in named categories, each with a clear start and end: `RUN STARTED` / `RUN ENDED` with the run's id, `SCREEN` (opened, drawn, left), and others, so the maintainer can send exactly the slice asked for after a run on his phone (a POCO X6 5G). In effect the Diagnostics screen (below), live and always on.

*Added 1 October 2026 (the maintainer, after the Wave A playtest):*
- **The globe, properly.** "Performance on my phone now tanks … scrolling down the about page [is] very laggy … the globe problem, since it is plaguing our app everywhere." Wave A's stopgap (P8.5-34): rings out of view are skipped, the view is rounded so a frame that matches the last one reuses its path (the hold costs nothing, the spin builds ~20 paths a second), and the About globe stops while the page scrolls, once it's scrolled away and under another screen. What's still wrong is the method. Every moving frame builds a path string of thousands of points in a worklet, hands it to react-native-svg, and the native side parses it and redraws it on the UI thread, the same thread the scroll and every touch run on. The draw's globe (`GlobeReveal`, placement) and the About globe (`SpinningGlobe`) both do it. **The real fix:**
  - **Measure first** on the maintainer's phone, with the Diagnostics screen's frame budget: UI-thread time a frame for each globe, spinning and zooming.
  - **Draw it without path strings.** `@shopify/react-native-skia` (works with Expo SDK 54; a native module, so a new dev build): the land as one `SkPath` built once per country in unit-sphere points, transformed per frame on the GPU, or a shader that samples an equirectangular land texture onto the sphere. Either way no string is built or parsed per frame. Skia also runs on the web through CanvasKit, a wasm download of a few megabytes: measure it and weigh it for the web build, or keep SVG on the web only.
  - **Or, cheapest, pre-render it.** The About globe's loop is the same every time: a sprite sheet or a short looping video made at build time from the same projection would cost a decoder and nothing else. The draw's globe can't (its country differs each run), so it needs the Skia route.
  - **Done when:** both globes hold 60 fps on the maintainer's phone, and scrolling About with the globe turning feels the same as scrolling a page without it.
- **Waiting on taps (P8.5-01).** The "Waiting…" plate works, but "it looks a little bad" on taps that have nothing to wait for. The plan: once the loads are measured, a plate shows the waiting look only when its action really takes time (a long load gets it straight away; a tap that finishes in under ~100 ms never shows it), "rather than some waiting time on all".
- **Some more stuff as well** (same note): profile everything on the list below with the globe fixed first, since a globe running in the background skews every other measurement.

**Why this phase exists.** The maintainer's standing complaint is that the app is slow on a phone: loading between screens, long waits before results, the stats screen regenerating a whole run before it shows anything, and the globe lagging (P8, globe). These are separate from the design work, so they get their own pass instead of being picked at during others.

**Scope**

- **Measure first.** Profile the real hot paths on a mid-range Android phone before changing anything. Lessons from The Dugout: profile before tuning, and a diagnosis isn't done until the number responds. The known suspects:
  - the full-run stats pass (`computeRunStats` regenerating every match sheet) on the verdict, Awards Night and the stats screen;
  - SQLite roster loads repeated per screen;
  - route transitions that mount a heavy screen before they paint;
  - the globe's per-frame SVG projection;
  - long tables rendered without virtualisation.
- **Compute once, keep it.** The run's stats should be computed once, when the run ends, and kept for every screen that needs them, rather than recomputed on each visit.
- **Loading you can see (maintainer, 19 September 2026; replaces the older "no spinner" line).**
  - **The problem:** most screens show nothing at all while they load.
  - **Every load shows it's loading:** a spinner, or another visual in the Kit Drop style that says so, never a blank screen.
  - **A deliberate pause:** once loading finishes, the content still waits a random 0.5–1 s before it appears, so each arrival feels deliberate rather than abrupt. Apply it to the screens that load, not to plain taps.
  - **Measure first:** the real load times still get measured, so the wait never hides a slow load.
- **The Diagnostics screen** from [`../diagnostics/`](../diagnostics/00-README.md): budgets with exact call sites, the self-test and engine fingerprint, the report format, and the tester moved under it. It is the instrument this phase is measured with, so it comes first in the phase.

**Done when** · every budget in the Diagnostics plan is met on the maintainer's phone; no screen transition waits on a full-run computation; the globe runs smoothly.

*(Built 6 October 2026, everything that can be built before the phone's first reading; what's left needs that reading or a native decision.*
- ***§1 Logs, everywhere.*** *One log ([`../diagnostics/`](../diagnostics/00-README.md), steps 1–5), now 1,000 lines, in categories with a start and end: `run` (RUN STARTED / RUN ENDED with a tag), `screen` (each screen and how long it took to draw), `perf` (every timed operation with its time and the memory in use after it), `ui` (every plate pressed by its label, every tab, a row tapped without its label since rows carry people's names). **On the phone every line also goes to Android's own log in every build**, starting `POM` and a timestamp, so a release build can be watched live from the PC: `adb logcat -s ReactNativeJS` in a VS Code terminal. The Diagnostics log filters by level, category and "the last run only", and shares exactly the lines shown: the slice the maintainer asks for.*
- ***The live match's 24 s v 15 s.*** *Found where the time can go: the clock (minutes × ms), a 0.9 s beat on each goal and red card, 0.5 s between periods, the shootout, and the time it stood still (paused, scrolled out of view, another screen on top). The full-time log line now breaks the total into exactly those, plus "other", so the next 24 s says which. Not changed: nothing is slow until that line says so.*
- ***Waiting on taps.*** *A plate goes held (pressed in, darker) at once, but "Waiting…" and its bar come only when the action is still running after 100 ms. Most taps never show them.*
- ***Loading you can see.*** *`GhostRows` in the kit, breathing outlines of the rows to come (still when the phone asks for less motion), on the friends list (which showed nothing at all), Clubs, a club, the chat, a profile and the career; Runs and Ranks keep their run-label skeletons. The deliberate pause (19 Sept) is a floor, not an extra wait: a first load appears no sooner than a random 0.5–1 s after it began, and a slower load isn't padded (`src/lib/loading.ts`), so it never hides a slow one. Eight screens.*
- ***The pundits' tournaments lag.*** *Opening them played every pundit's whole tournament on the first frame. Now the panel's is drawn at once and each pundit's follows, one a frame, filling in the rail (`pundits:build`, timed).*
- ***Switching tabs mid-simulation.*** *Measured, not changed: every tab switch is timed to the frame that shows it (`ui:tab`) and logged, so the phone's reading says which screen and how long.*
- ***Compute once, keep it.*** *Already so since Wave F (one `liveRunData`, the stats screen and Awards Night read the cache). Closed the gap left: two callers at once shared nothing until the first finished; they share the one in flight now, and a new run can't be handed the last run's stats.*
- ***Roster loads repeated.*** *The read-only rosters are read once a session per club-season and handed out as copies (`query:rosters`, timed on a miss).*
- ***Long lists (N-14).*** *Runs (up to 100 runs) is a `FlatList` with its title and filters as the header. Ranks pages 50 at a time, and the 36-club tables sit inside scrolling pages where a virtual list buys nothing, so they stay.*
- ***Loops nobody sees.*** *The draft stays mounted under the whole run (it opens the next screen with push); its curse scramble and its omen's flicker now stop when it isn't in front. The globes, the live match and the Deep Match already stopped.*
- ***Already met, closed:** the Deep Match's formation already uses the shared pitch (`MatchLineupPitch` on the kit's `Pitch`, as the match sheet); the web database already loads behind Home, with the league screen saying so.*
- ***New budgets:** `ui:tab`, `screen:result` (the audit's result-screen mount, 04 §2), `pundits:build`, `query:rosters`; 35 runtime keys.*
- ***Waiting on the phone's first reading (P9-14):** the globes' frames (`frame:globe`) decide the redraw; the 24 s match; `ui:tab`; every provisional target. **Native decisions, 6 Oct (the maintainer):** `@shopify/react-native-skia` 2.2.12 is in the 9.5 build, unused until the globe's redraw (a native module can't ship over the air later); orientation and predictive back stay as they are; `expo`, `expo-constants` and `expo-file-system` moved to SDK 54's patch versions. N-16 (one globe drawing) goes with the redraw: both globes already share one module's land, projection and clipping.*
- *Left out, on purpose: caching the last few match sheets ([`10-ADAPT-OPTIMIZE-A11Y.md`](10-ADAPT-OPTIMIZE-A11Y.md) §3.2) until a reading says sheets are slow; splitting the season screens further is Phase 9.75's (step 4b's note). Checklist P9-15 to P9-22.)*

## Phase 9.5 · Check everything on a phone (added 1 October 2026)

From Wave D on, the maintainer tests on the web only: each native build costs a free EAS build and about two hours, so features that need one are built, typechecked and script-verified, and wait. Every such item is written down as it's built in [`../PHASE-9.5-CHECKLIST.md`](../PHASE-9.5-CHECKLIST.md), with its wave and date, plus what to run first (SQL files, the edge function, the native build). Phase 9.5 is one native build after Phase 9, the maintainer going down that list on the phone, and fixing everything that isn't marked as working. The web build didn't have the phone's performance problems, which is why the phone pass waits until Phase 9's performance work is in.

## Phase 9.75 · Centralisation, round three, and a full audit (added 3 October 2026)

*The maintainer, 3 October 2026:* after Phase 9.5 and before Phase 10, "a full centralization audit again and do it, plus full app thing."

**Goal.** Everything Phases 9 and 9.5 added or changed is held to the same rule as phase two: one base, only extend. Then the whole app is audited again, not just what drifted.

**Scope.**
- A third centralisation audit across every screen and mode, the way [`../centralisation/11-RE-AUDIT-2.md`](../centralisation/11-RE-AUDIT-2.md) re-measured the first: every item re-counted, new items numbered on from the last, and a plan in steps.
- **Then build it**, step by step, each behind a check that failed first.
- **A full app audit** alongside it, the kind Wave G was ([`../audit-2026-10/00-README.md`](../audit-2026-10/00-README.md)): measured first, logic, security, performance, the interface re-scored.

**Done when** · the audit's steps are built and checked; the full audit's findings are fixed or written down with a reason; the checklist has a row for everything built.

### Phase 9.5's results (the maintainer, 7 October 2026, first dev build on the POCO X6)

The checklist went through on the phone ([`../PHASE-9.5-CHECKLIST.md`](../PHASE-9.5-CHECKLIST.md), statuses written there). Most of it works ("it looks better", Phase 9 "všetko funguje, a pekne"); these are what didn't, and they come **first** in 9.75, before the centralisation audit. Most of them are on the web too, so each fix is for both.

**P9.75-01 · The live match on the season screen in light mode.** "The live match thing in season normal isn't applied for light mode, it just does not have the other component. Other screens as well." The live match card keeps its dark (floodlit) look, or loses a part, on the everyday ground in light mode, on the season screen and others. Find every live-match surface and check both grounds. *(Done 8 Oct 2026, step 3: `LiveMatch` takes the screen's ground; `verify-grounds` lists the pins that stay. Checklist 975-7.)*

**P9.75-02 · The World Cup spin has no flags** (S1-3 failed on the phone; it passed on the web on 3 Oct). The draft's World Cup spin card shows empty where the nation's flag should be. *(Done 8 Oct 2026, step 3: the reel keyed by club, not season; `verify-spin`. Checklist 975-9.)*

**P9.75-03 · Subs look different from the starting eleven, and swapping from the bench is hard to read.** "The subs don't have the things as the players in the starting eleven, make it the same; hard to see swapping from bench, like where you can swap to." A bench player's tag should carry what a starter's does, and while a bench player is picked, the slots they can go into must be marked. *(Done 8 Oct 2026, step 7: one hanger builder; the bench carries the mark and position; what can't take the held player steps back. Checklist 975-18.)*

**P9.75-04 · The knockouts' press is at the bottom of a long scroll.** "Why do I have to scroll all the way down, just put it so you can just switch like a normal person." The press under the knockouts becomes a tab beside the rounds, the way the table and group stages have it (the Press tab). *(Done 8 Oct 2026, step 6: Rounds · Bracket · Press. Checklist 975-16.)*

**P9.75-05 · You can't find your own knockouts.** "If you were in the Europa League you cannot find it, or at least I can't." On the full path (and wherever your competition isn't the first one shown), your own competition's knockout phase isn't reachable, or not visibly. *(Done 8 Oct 2026, step 6: the Europe tab lists your competition first; the Bracket tab names it. Checklist 975-17.)*

**P9.75-06 · Offline runs never go up (D-1, D-2, D-3 failed).** D-1: a run finished offline does say queued, "but it doesn't seem to appear again. Stuck somewhere." It never reached Supabase or Runs. D-2: turning the network back on doesn't send it. D-3: reopening the app online doesn't either. The queue (`src/lib/runQueue.ts`, `flushSavedRuns` in `src/db/queries/runs.ts`) needs tracing on the phone with the log: whether a flush starts at all (online events, the foreground event, start-up), what `send` answers (the queue only sends under the account that played the run, and drops a run the server refuses with a log line), and whether the run is still in storage. D-4 (the date) worked. *(Done 8 Oct 2026, step 2: every queue step logged, the sign-in race, a 20 s send limit, refused runs kept and shown. The phone's retest decides it. Checklist 975-4 to 975-6.)*

**P9.75-07 · The keyboard covers the clubs' invite field.** On inviting someone to a club, the keyboard sits over the field (as the chat's did before P8.5-06). *(Done 8 Oct 2026, step 5: `KitScreen` keeps every field above the keyboard; `verify-diag` rule 2e. Checklist 975-14.)*

**P9.75-08 · One Slovak slip.** A pundit, Pavúk, shows his country as "Croatia", not "Chorvátsko": one place prints a pundit's country without `countryName()`. *(Done 8 Oct 2026, step 3: "Pavúk" is the bracket; 22 lines in 13 files printed names raw, now through `countryName()`, and `verify-i18n` fails on a new one. Checklist 975-8.)*

**P9.75-09 · The press needs another pass.** S7 works, "but needs another pass, since the stories are not handmade": the cup stories read generated. A writing pass on every story kind, by hand, in both languages. *(Done 8 Oct 2026, step 9: three or more variants for every kind and branch, the cups' standfirsts too, turning with the matchday. Checklist 975-22.)*

**P9.75-10 · Achievement toasts (C-3) couldn't be confirmed.** Nothing was earned during the session. Check on the next build, or with a run that earns one.

**From the phone's first reading** ([`../PERF-LOG.md`](../PERF-LOG.md), 7 Oct, a development build, so slower than release):
- **P9.75-11 · The run hub's Stats tab freezes the phone for 3.9 to 4.7 s** (`ui:tab` 3,856 and 4,692 ms, stalls of 3,775 and 4,557 ms on `/game/run`). The worst thing measured, by far. The Teams tab took 1.8 s, and switching back to Table once 2.6 s. *(Built 8 Oct 2026, step 4: `BoardList` mounts a page of rows at a time; `stats:board` and `hub:teams` time the rest. Waits for the phone's reading. Checklist 975-11.)*
- **P9.75-12 · Opening the run hub takes 0.8 to 1.6 s** (`ui:navigate` 778–1,631 ms, budget 150/350), with a 0.9–2.4 s stall as it mounts. *(Built 8 Oct 2026, step 4: header first, body a frame later; the knockout worked out once. Checklist 975-12.)*
- **P9.75-13 · A cup run's stats take 1.7 s and freeze the result screen for 1.4 s** (`stats:ucl` 1,656 ms with a 1,446 ms stall). World Cup stats 0.7–0.85 s. *(Built 8 Oct 2026, step 4: the pass gives the screen a turn every 20 sheets, D2. Checklist 975-13.)*
- **P9.75-14 · The JS heap grows from 20 MB to 104 MB in a 10-minute session and never falls**, in steps of about 8–16 MB each time the run hub's tabs open. Could be caches doing their job or a leak: measure it in a release build first.
- **P9.75-15 · Smaller:** a draft spin 448 ms (budget 150/400), and 270–360 ms stalls on opening mode select, difficulty and formation.

*Audited 7 Oct 2026 (documents only, no code):* [`../audit-9.75/00-README.md`](../audit-9.75/00-README.md). Every P9.75 item traced to its cause (7 confirmed, the offline queue partly, 5 hypotheses with their probes), centralisation round three ([`../centralisation/13-RE-AUDIT-3.md`](../centralisation/13-RE-AUDIT-3.md), R3-01 to -13), the independent audit (logic, security, the interface re-scored with the phone's evidence: 32/40), and one build order in ten steps ([`06-BUILD-ORDER.md`](../audit-9.75/06-BUILD-ORDER.md)). Before coding: check `submit-run`'s deployed version on the dashboard.

**Then the order.** (1) P9.75-01 to -15, each fix behind a check where one can be written; (2) the centralisation audit, round three, as the scope above says; (3) **the independent audit**, as usual and from scratch: the whole app looked at as if for the first time, deliberately not working from the earlier documents' lists, to find what they don't (the maintainer, 7 Oct: "completely independent of everything, to find issues or other stuff"). Measured first, logic, security, performance, the interface re-scored, then its findings fixed or written down with a reason.

## Phase 10 · The landing page (added 19 September 2026)

*(27 September 2026: P8-154's website brief, [`docs/website/WEBSITE-BRIEF.md`](../website/WEBSITE-BRIEF.md), is this phase's creative brief: the live counter, the design bar and the prompts are there. Where this section and the brief differ, the brief is newer.)*

*(9 October 2026, Phase 10 began: the website set was re-audited and is now the plan, newer than this section and the brief wherever they differ: [`docs/website/00-README.md`](../website/00-README.md). What changed here: the site is **Astro** in `landing/` on Node 22, not plain HTML; it stays on **`vercel.app`** (Q1), so the domain and `play.` below wait for a bought domain; the hero is **a live spin** and the page is [`09-LANDING-SHAPE.md`](../website/09-LANDING-SHAPE.md); the site speaks the **legal flavour** (generic competition names, altered club names, no crests); **1.0.0 comes after Phase 11**, not with this phase, so the site launches with the game in early access; the counter is a polled `site_counters` row; `latest.json` stays on the game's address; the audit's additions are [`10-AUDIT-ADDENDUM.md`](../website/10-AUDIT-ADDENDUM.md). Fact-check: [`07-FACT-CHECK.md`](../website/07-FACT-CHECK.md).)*

**Goal.** Someone who has never heard of the game lands on one page and, in ten seconds, knows what it is, wants to try it, and can: play in the browser or get it on Android. Today the web address opens straight into the app's Home, which assumes you already know.

*Added 29 September 2026, the maintainer:*

- **1.0.0 is the whole thing.** ~~The app is called 1.0.0 only once Phases 8.5, 9 and this one (the landing page) are done and the finishing work is in (decided 29 September 2026).~~ **Changed 9 October 2026: 1.0.0 comes after Phase 11** (the PC sweep and the UI overhaul now follow this phase). Until then the versions stay under 1 (0.9.0 is Phase 8, built; see the version history in `src/data/versionHistory.ts`). It ships under a football release name with a welcome update ([`../website/05-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`](../website/05-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md) Q13).
- **The new logo.** A new mark, "an overall improvement on the current one": a volt-green triangle over a red one, the wordmark between them in white, and the orange pin at the top right. Files: `assets/Group 3.png` and the original `Group 3.svg` (the maintainer's export, 533 × 528, fills `#D5FF3F`, `#E1141F`, `#FF5A00`, white and black). The site and the app's icon, splash and wordmark move to it; the white wordmark needs a dark ground or an ink version for cotton.
- **Upgrade the vibecode-audit skill first.** Before the site is audited, update and extend the `vibecode-audit` skill itself so its security part is properly researched and truly thorough: real rate limiting, row-level security, the admin area, abuse of forms. Then run it against the site.
- **A community and a questions section**, kept quieter than the landing page itself: the plan is [`../website/`](../website/00-README.md).

**Where it lives (a decision for the maintainer).**

- **Recommended: its own small static site on the main domain**, with the game at `play.` (e.g. `perfectionormisery.com` and `play.perfectionormisery.com`).
  - The landing page stays tiny, fast and fully indexable. The app keeps `/` as Home and nothing in its routing changes.
  - It's a second Vercel project from the same repo (a `landing/` folder, plain HTML and CSS, no framework) with the same fonts and tokens. Meaning links to run need to change.

**What's on it.** One page, read top to bottom, in the Kit Drop style and on the final logo (P8-74):

1. **The poster.** The logo, the one-line promise ("Draft an XI from random real club-seasons. Survive a season. Perfection or Misery."), and two plates: Play in your browser, Get it on Android.
2. **How a run goes**, in four frames: the spin, the draw, the season strip, the verdict label. Each is a real screenshot from the app, never a mock-up passed off as the real thing.
3. **The verdicts:** the tier ladder as a row of garment labels, from PERFECTION to ABSOLUTE MISERY, so the stakes read at a glance.
4. **The modes:** league, All Time, Chaos, Cursed, Champions League, the full path and the World Cup, each on its colourway tape.
5. **Proof it's deep:** Awards Night, the pundits, the press and the match sheet, shown in two or three screenshots at most.
6. **The ranks,** live if cheap: the top five runs this week, read from `runs` (public read under `policies.sql`). Otherwise leave it out, and never fake it.
7. **The footer:** Privacy, Terms, the version (P8-73), "Made in Slovakia", and a contact.

**Engineering.**

- Static HTML: under 100 KB before images, images in AVIF/WebP, no client JavaScript except the optional live ranks.
- Full metadata: title, description, canonical URL, an Open Graph image built from the logo and a verdict label, `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, `llms.txt`.
- The vibecode audit (`02-VIBECODE-AUDIT.md`) run against it before launch; it must pass every blocker.
- Link previews: the Open Graph image is the one place a pasted link shows the brand, so it gets designed, not generated.
- **Measured on real visitors (P8-179):** Web Analytics and Speed Insights switched on for the landing page's own Vercel project, with the plain-HTML snippet the dashboard gives (the only scripts besides the optional live ranks). Lighthouse is the lab test before launch; Speed Insights is the same page on real phones afterwards; both get recorded. The two plates are measured without paid events: "Play in your browser" shows up in the app's own analytics as the landing page's referrer, and "Get it on Android" on the store's side. The whole setup: [`docs/Web Analytics & Speed Insights.md`](<../Web%20Analytics%20%26%20Speed%20Insights.md>) §5.

**Needs first.** The final logo and the win/loss colour (P8-74), a domain (Phase 6), the redesigned screens to screenshot (Phases 7–8), and the Play Store listing if "Get it on Android" links there. Until the listing exists, that plate points to the APK or says "coming soon", never to a dead link.

**Done when** · a stranger can say what the game is after ten seconds; Lighthouse scores 95+ on every category on a throttled mobile profile; Speed Insights shows real visitors' P75 LCP, INP and CLS all "good" (2.5 s, 200 ms, 0.1) once there are enough visits to say; the vibecode scan passes; a pasted link shows the designed preview in WhatsApp and Discord.

**Commands** · `/impeccable shape`, `/impeccable craft`, `/impeccable critique`; the vibecode audit.

## Phase 10.5 · The PC sweep (added 3 October 2026)

*The maintainer, 3 October 2026:* after Phase 10, "a full sweep of the app (on the pc) so it works there."

**Goal.** The game works properly on a computer, in the browser: every screen, every mode, wide windows, mouse and keyboard, not just a phone layout stretched.

**Scope.** Every screen walked on a desktop browser at several window sizes. Wide layouts (panes, the bracket beside the rounds, tables), hover and focus states, keyboard use (Tab order, Enter, Escape, the arrow keys the league season already takes), scroll and zoom, text that's too small or lines that run too long, anything that only worked by touch. A checklist in the Phase 9.5 style, then the fixes.

**Guests, rethought (added 9 October 2026, decided by the maintainer):** option C of [`../release/09-GUESTS.md`](../release/09-GUESTS.md). No session until sign-up (no anonymous account per browser or install), sign-up as a plain account, and a guest's last 10 finished runs kept on the phone and sent through the run queue once the account exists, so *Keep my runs* is true. Then "require current password when updating" is turned on, and the old guest accounts are deleted once. Its done-when list is that document's §5.

**Done when** · the maintainer has played a run of every mode on the PC, nothing on the list is still open, and the guest checks of [`../release/09-GUESTS.md`](../release/09-GUESTS.md) §5 pass.

## Phase 11 · The UI/UX overhaul, second pass (added 3 October 2026)

*The maintainer, 3 October 2026:* after even the PC sweep, "where we literally go and upgrade our UI/UX and everything."

**Goal.** A real upgrade of how the game looks and feels, on phone and PC, not a consistency pass.

**How.**
- **impeccable's critique** first ([`01-CRITIQUE.md`](01-CRITIQUE.md) is the first round; [`../audit-2026-10/05-UI-RESCORE.md`](../audit-2026-10/05-UI-RESCORE.md) the last score): scored, screen by screen, before anything changes.
- **Look for other skills** that would help (design, motion, accessibility), and say which and why.
- **Ask the maintainer what they have.** They keep saved videos of UI/UX upgrades; at the start of this phase, ask specifically for them and work from them as references.
- The Kit Drop direction ([`../../DESIGN.md`](../../DESIGN.md)) stays the identity unless the maintainer decides otherwise.
- **The match sheet's redesign** (P8-48, and what centralisation step 8 left of C-15): its 121 interim `ScaleText` lines onto `KitText`, a context table that marks both sides of a match (extend `LeagueTable`, or decide the sheet's table is a different thing), and the knockout road with a button per leg (extend `TieRow`). [`../centralisation/12-PHASE-TWO-FINAL.md`](../centralisation/12-PHASE-TWO-FINAL.md) step 8.

- **The pundits in the cups** (the maintainer, 9 October 2026: "in league they work fine"). In a league the pundits predict a table you then play, and the verdict checks it. In the World Cup and the European cups they play out a whole tournament from a draw of their own (P8-165), which never matches the real one: since 9 Oct the real draw comes first (P9.75-32), so their groups and bracket now sit beside yours and disagree with it. Rethink what a cup's pundits predict: on the real draw (changes the verdict's replay, `cup-calls.ts`), only rounds and a champion, or something else; decided with the maintainer at the start of the phase.

**Done when** · decided at the start of the phase, with the maintainer, from the critique's scores.

### After Phase 11 · The launch videos, redone (added 4 October 2026)

A first pair was made on 3–4 October 2026 (`D:\Perfection or Misery\brag-output\`: `teaser.mp4`, `trailer.mp4`, `brag-plan.md` with the storyboards and how to re-record). *The maintainer, 4 October:* "great videos … the music slaps", but they get redone "with much more thought" once the game is truly finished, because the UI overhaul will change every screen in them. The format is right but "can and should be better".

**The brief, as the maintainer answered it (3 Oct 2026), to reuse:**

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| What for | **Two videos:** a **social teaser** (TikTok / Reels / Shorts) and a **website / store trailer** |
| Feel | **Cinematic football trailer**: big type, dramatic cuts, the verdict as the climax |
| Shape | **Vertical 9:16** (1080×1920) |
| Footage | **The real web build, recorded**, and any screen that doesn't look presentable on the web build gets **fixed in the app first**, not hidden |

**Carry forward:**
- Same two videos, same format, rebuilt on the overhauled screens.
- **Music:** keep the energy, but some parts should be "a lot better"; try other instruments rather than the current synth palette. The score is generated (`video/synth.mjs`); a recorded or sample-based track is fair game.
- Tools: the `brag` / `brag-slim` and `remotion-motion-graphics` skills; the traps from the first round are in `brag-plan.md` (screencast tearing while scrolling, the dev server's file limit, the World Cup test tool's weak auto-draft).
- App Store previews must be 15–30 s, so plan a ≤30 s store cut beside the website trailer.

## Keeping these documents alive

Documentation that stops matching the app is worse than none. Four habits keep this set honest.

1. **Each phase ends by updating the documents it touched.** A screen that ships gets its wireframe replaced by a real screenshot and an "as built" line recording anything that changed from the plan and why.
2. **Decisions go in the log** in [`00-README.md`](00-README.md), dated, with the reason. A decision made in a chat that isn't in the log didn't happen.
3. **Every document carries a status** at the top: draft, provisional, locked, or as built.
4. **A short playtest note after each phase.** Two or three friends play a run on their own phones; the note records what they did, where they hesitated and what they said out loud. A screen recording of one run per phase goes with it. The critique and audit scores measure the interface; the playtest measures the game.
